Taken as "twice as much" it says the same thing, that there is more to wisdom than Job has seen, so nothing is noted.
- hishshah (11:6; 39:17) "let be forgotten": the causative of nashah "forget", with God as its subject both times. yashsheh lekha eloah me'awonekha "God lets some of your guilt be forgotten for you": min is "some of". The other verb with these letters, a creditor's ("God exacts from you less than your guilt"), takes be- of the debtor (Deut 15:2) and is not noted, since both say that Job is not held to the whole of his guilt. weda after the wish (11:6) "And know".
- cheqer eloah (11:7) "what is to be searched out in God", cheqer staying beside chaqar "search out" (5:9; 8:8; 9:10); takhlit "limit" (26:10; 28:3, where the two words stand together again). matsa "find", twice, as in the Hebrew; with ad "find one's way to". The other construction ("Can you find God by searching, or find out the Almighty to the limit?") says the same thing and is not noted.
- govah "height"; amoq "deep"; arokh "long"; rachav "broad"; middah "measure". The feminine "it" of 11:8-9 points back to the limit of the Almighty (or to wisdom), and "It is" is supplied twice in 11:8. pa'al stays "do" (7:20). She'ol "Sheol" and shamayim "the heavens", as listed.
- 11:10 answers 9:11-12 in Job's own words, and the English repeats them: chalaf "sweep on", mi yeshivennu "who can turn him back?" hisgir with no object (11:10) "shut someone in", beside sagar "shut" (3:10; 12:14); with a person handed to others (16:11) "hand over" may fit. hiqhil "call an assembly".
- metei shaw (11:11; Ps 26:4) "empty men": shaw stays "emptiness" (7:3), so that it is heard with "a hollow man" (11:12). hu with a finite verb "he himself", as listed. hitbonen "look closely (at)" (23:15; 30:20; 31:1; 37:14), kept distinct from "consider" (sim lev) and "discern" (bin). 11:11b "and will he not look closely at it?" [alt: he sees harm without having to look closely] (decision log, call: close). The wayyiqtol is put in the English present, as at 5:15.
- navuv "hollow" (Exod 27:8). yillavev (11:12; the form stands only here) "get sense": the verb is made from levav "heart", and "understanding" is left for binah; one bracketed note says so, and that it sounds like "hollow". ayir "colt"; pere "wild donkey", as at 6:5; adam "a human being", as at 5:7. 11:12 "a hollow man will get sense when a wild donkey's colt is born a human being" [alt: Yet a hollow man can get sense, though a human being is born a wild donkey's colt] (decision log, call: ask). The "and" that sets a picture beside a saying is "when" here ("as" at 5:7).
- hekhin lev (11:13) "set one's heart right" (Ps 78:8): the full form of the idiom that is "set one's mind on" at 8:8. paras kaf el "spread out one's palm to": kaf stays "palm" (9:30), beside "in your hand" (11:14). attah with a finite verb (11:13, 16) "you yourself", as listed. 11:13-15 is one sentence, with 11:14 between dashes; ki az (11:15) opens the main clause, "surely then" (8:6 "surely now").
- hirchiq "put far away". hishkin "let live", beside shakan "live" (4:19). ohalim "tents", plural as in the Hebrew. awen stays "harm" and awlah "injustice".
- nasa panim (11:15; 22:26) "lift one's face": Zophar answers "I cannot lift my head" (10:15). mum "blemish": mimmum "without blemish", the same English as amomos. mutsaq (11:15) "solid": the word is for metal that has been cast (37:18; compare 41:23-24). lo tira "you will not be afraid", as at 5:21.
- amal stays "misery" (11:16); shakhach "forget" (8:13); avar of water "pass away", as the Job 6 line planned; zakar "remember".
- cheled (11:17) "life": the span of a life (Ps 39:5); it shares its English with chayyim, and no "your" is added. qum "rise"; mitstsohorayim "brighter than noon", "brighter" supplied. ta'ufah (11:17) "though it grows black": the verb of efah "blackness" (10:22), so that Zophar is heard answering Job's last words. boqer "morning".
- batach with no object (11:18) "trust", as at 6:20; betach "security": lavetach "in security" ("safety" stays for yesha). chafar (11:18) "look around": the verb is "dig" (3:21) and so "search" (39:29; Deut 1:22; Josh 2:2-3). The same letters as a form of chafer "be humiliated" (6:20) are not noted: in a row of promises the sentence does not allow it.
- ravats of a person at rest (11:19) "bed down": the word for animals lying in their place (Isa 17:2; Zeph 3:13), kept distinct from shakhav "lie down"; "crouch" stays for Gen 4:7. en macharid "there will be no one to make you tremble" (Lev 26:6; Mic 4:4): hecherid "make tremble", kept distinct from bi'et "terrify" and chittat "frighten". chillah fene "seek the favor of" (Prov 19:6): the idiom is translated by its meaning. rabbim "many".
- kalah of eyes (11:20; 17:5) "fail"; elsewhere it stays "come to an end". manos "refuge"; avad min (11:20; Ps 142:4; Amos 2:14) "be lost to", where "perish" does not fit; the perfect is put in the English present. mappach nefesh (only here) "the breathing out of life" (the verb with nefesh at 31:39; Jer 15:9): left as open as the Hebrew (their only hope is to die, or their hope dies away); gava stays "breathe one's last".

## Settled in Job 12
- Hebrew text: the Masoretic Text; the chapter has no Qere. The main text keeps to the Masoretic vowels: 12:18 musar "the discipline of kings", and the same letters read as the word for bonds are the alt (decision log, call: close). Not noted: 12:9, where a few Hebrew copies have "God" for "Yahweh" (the name stands only here in the speeches of Job and his friends; the sense is the same); 12:23, where the old Greek has "leads nations astray" for "makes nations grow" (the same consonants); and what the old Greek puts differently (12:4-6), as in Job 2 to 11.
- Job's fourth speech is 12:2-14:22: chapter 12 ends with no closing quotation mark, as the Job 4 line says. "You" is plural in 12:2-3 (the friends) and singular in 12:7-8; English does not show the change, and nothing is added (the Job 7 line).
- omnam ki (12:2) "Truly", as at 9:2. am with no article (12:2) "the people". levav and lev of what a person thinks with (12:3, 24) "sense", so that Job is heard answering Zophar's "get sense" (11:12); "heart" stays elsewhere. nafal min (12:3; 13:2) "fall short of", keeping "fall". et-mi en (12:3) "who does not have": et of what a person has in him, like immadi (9:35). kemo-elleh "such things as these".
- sechoq of a person (12:4) "a laughingstock", beside "laughter" (8:21). The third person inside the first-person sentence is kept ("I am one who is a laughingstock to his friend"). qara le- "call to". The participle and its wayyiqtol (qore ... wayya'anehu) are put in the past, "one who called to God, and he answered him": the days of 29:2-5. tsaddiq "righteous"; tamim "blameless", the same English as tam (two forms of one word, like tom / tummah).
- 12:5: the first word is read as la- with pid, "for misfortune" [alt: a despised torch] (decision log, call: close). pid (12:5; 30:24; 31:29) "misfortune", leaving "calamity" for ed (18:12; 21:17, 30; 31:3, 23). buz "contempt" (12:5, 21; 31:34). ashtut (only here) "thought". sha'anan (12:5) "one who is untroubled" and shalah (12:6) "be at ease" stand in neighboring lines and stay distinct, as the Job 3 line says. nakhon le- "ready for" (15:23; 18:12). ma'ad "slip": mo'ade ragel "those whose foot slips".
- shoded (12:6; 15:21) "destroyer", beside shod "destruction" (5:21-22): Job is heard answering "your tent is at peace" (5:24). battuchot (only here) "security", the same English as betach (11:18): he answers Zophar too. hirgiz with God as its object (12:6) "provoke" ("shake" at 9:6).
- 12:6 la'asher hevi eloah beyado "for the one who brings a god in his hand" [alt: for the one into whose hand God brings it] (decision log, call: ask). hevi "bring"; the perfect is put in the English present, as at 9:5-10. eloah is "a god" here, lowercase, as the same word is "his god" in Hab 1:11; "who brings God in his hand" is the same construction and is not noted.
- behemot of the animals as a whole, beside birds and fish (12:7; 18:3; 35:11) "beasts"; "livestock" stays where they are set beside wild animals (Gen 1:24-26). she'al-na ... wetorekka (12:7) "ask ... now, and they will teach you", the English of 8:8-10: Job turns Bildad's words around. A singular verb with a collective subject (12:7) is "they". of hashamayim "the birds of the sky" and dege hayyam "the fish of the sea", as in Gen 1:26. siach le- (12:8) "speak to" ("complain" stays for 7:11): the feminine "it will teach you" goes with "the earth", so the same letters as the noun "shrub" (30:4, 7) are not noted. sipper "recount" (15:17; 28:27), kept distinct from higgid "tell"; its object ("it") is supplied.
- 12:9 bekhol-elleh "among all these": the creatures just named. "By all these", on the pattern of "by this you will know that" (Exod 7:17), is not noted, since either way the creatures are the witnesses. yad YHWH asetah zot "the hand of Yahweh has done this", the words of Isa 41:20. asher beyado (12:10) is made a sentence of its own, as at 5:5.
- nefesh kol chay (12:10) "the life of every living thing". ruach of the life in a person (12:10; 10:12) "spirit", leaving "breath" for neshamah (the two stand together at 27:3; 34:14). kol besar ish "all human flesh".
- ozen "ear"; bachan "test", as listed; ta'am (verb) "taste"; lo (12:11) "for itself". The "and" that sets a picture beside a saying is "as" (5:7); the line returns at 34:3. ta'am (noun) of people (12:20) "discernment", with one bracketed note that it is the word for "taste" (12:11).
- yashish "the aged" (12:12; 15:10; 29:8; 32:6); zaqen (12:20; 32:9) "elder". Keep the two distinct. orekh yamim "length of days". tevunah "understanding", the same English as binah (two nouns from one root). 12:12 is put as a statement, with be- carried over to its second half ("in length of days") [alt: Is wisdom among the aged ...?] (decision log, call: close). be- of people "among"; immo (12:13, 16) "with him".
- gevurah "might" (it shares its English with kratos). oz (12:16; 26:2) "power", leaving "strength" for koach, which stands beside it at 26:2. etsah "counsel" and tushiyyah (12:16) "sound wisdom", as planned. shogeg umashgeh "the one who goes astray and the one who leads astray", like shagah (6:24).
- haras "tear down"; niphal banah after it "be rebuilt". sagar al "shut (someone) in", like hisgir (11:10); niphal patach "be opened". atsar be- "hold back", as at 4:2; yavesh "dry up"; shillach "send out"; hafakh "overturn" (9:5). The imperfects of 12:14-15 are put in the English present; the participles of 12:17-24 are made sentences, as at 5:12 and 9:5-10, and the wayyiqtols that carry them on are in the present too (5:15).
- holikh (12:17, 19) "lead away": it shares its English with wayyanchem (12:23). sholal (12:17, 19; Mic 1:8, beside "naked") "stripped"; "barefoot" is the same picture of captives (Isa 20:2-4) and is not noted. holel (12:17; Isa 44:25; Eccl 7:7) "drive mad", not softened to "make a fool of".
- pittach "undo" (12:18; 39:5), the same English as lyo; rippah (12:21) "loosen" (hirpah stays "let go of"). asar "bind" (chavash stays "bind up"). ezor "loincloth"; motnayim (12:18) "waist"; meziach (only here; compare Ps 109:19) "belt".
- kohen "priest". etan (12:19) "long-established": what lasts and does not fail (Num 24:21; Jer 5:15). sillef "overthrow" (Prov 13:6; 21:12), kept distinct from hafakh "overturn" and iwwet "pervert".
- hesir "remove" (12:20, 24), as listed. safah of what is said (12:20) "speech": the idiom ("remove the lip") is translated by its meaning. ne'eman "trusted" (he'emin be- stays "put trust in"). laqach with ta'am (12:20) "take away".
- shafakh "pour out" (it shares its English with natakh). nadiv "noble". afiqim (12:21) "the strong", the pair of "nobles": not afiq "channel" (6:15), which has the same letters.
- gillah "uncover" (20:27; 33:16; 41:13). amuqot "deep things", beside amoq "deep" (11:8). hotsi la'or "bring out to the light"; tsalmawet "the shadow of death", as listed.
- hisgi (12:23) "make grow", beside sagah "grow" (8:7, 11). ibbed (piel) "destroy" (it shares its English with apollymi in the active). goy "nation". shatach "spread out". wayyanchem (12:23) "leads them away" [alt: guides them] (decision log, call: clear): nachah is "lead", and will be "guide" at 31:18 and 38:32.
- rosh of a leader (12:24) "chief". am ha'arets (12:24) "the people of the earth". hit'ah (12:24, 25) "make wander", the same English both times, also of the drunk man, and kept distinct from "lead astray" (12:16). betohu lo-darekh "in a waste with no way", tohu as the Job 6 line planned. mishshesh "grope", as at 5:14: Job turns Eliphaz's words about the shrewd (5:12-14) on counselors, judges and kings. welo-or "and without light", as at 4:21. shikkor "a drunk man".

## Settled in Job 13
- Hebrew text: the Masoretic Text, read by its Ketiv. 13:15: the Ketiv lo "not" is followed ("I will not wait"), and the Qere lo "for him" is the alt ("I will wait for him"), with an entry in the decision log (call: ask), as at 6:21. The main text keeps to the Masoretic vowels and letters. Not noted: 13:14, where the old Greek has no "Why" (with it or without it Job is putting his life in his hand); 13:12, the reading of gabbekhem as "your answers", a sense the word has nowhere in Scripture; 13:28, where the old Greek has "like a wineskin" for "like something rotten"; and what the old Greek puts differently (13:12, 27), as in Job 2 to 12.
- Job's fourth speech goes on (12:2-14:22): the chapter opens with a quotation mark and ends with none, as the Job 4 line says. "You" is plural in 13:2-17 (the friends); from 13:20 Job speaks to God ("you" is singular). English does not show the change, and nothing is added (the Job 7 line). 13:28 turns to "he" without naming anyone, and the English leaves it so (compare 12:4); 14:1 goes on with "a human being".
- kol alone (13:1) "it all". bin le- "discern", as listed. keda'tekhem yada'ti gam-ani (13:2) "What you know, I myself also know". 13:2b repeats 12:3 word for word, and the English repeats it.
- ulam ani with an imperfect (13:3) "But I myself would", the English of 5:8: Job is heard answering Eliphaz's "But I myself would seek God". we'ulam attem (13:4) "But you yourselves".
- hokhiach, continued: where a person argues his case (13:3, 15) "argue", and tokhachat (13:6; 23:4) "argument"; of God reproving (13:10) it stays "correct" (5:17). The echo between the two is not shown. hokhiach derakhay el-panav (13:15) "argue my ways to his face".
- tafal (13:4; 14:17; Ps 119:69) "plaster": tofle-shaqer "you plaster with lies", the participle made a verb, with no object added. sheqer "lies" (shaw stays "emptiness"). rofe "healer", beside rafa "heal" (5:18); elil "worthless": rofe'e elil "worthless healers", a noun of quality made an adjective, as at 8:6.
- mi-yitten with an infinitive absolute (13:5) "If only you would actually", as at 6:2. utehi lakhem lechokhmah "and that would be wisdom for you".
- hiqshiv "pay attention to" (33:31), beside shama "hear" and he'ezin "listen to". rivot (13:6) "contentions", beside riv "contend"; riv le- (13:8; Judg 6:31) "contend for"; riv im (13:19) "contend with", as listed.
- A phrase set first in a question (13:7, 8) "Is it for God that ...". remiyyah (13:7; 27:4) "deceit" (it shares its English with dolos). hetel be- (13:9) "fool" (verb), kept distinct from remiyyah and from "deceive" (Gen 3:13); hatulim (17:2) will need a wording of its own.
- nasa panim with another person's face (13:8, 10; 32:21; 34:19) "show favoritism to": the idiom is translated by its meaning, as with thaumazein prosopa; of one's own face it stays "lift one's face" (11:15; 22:26). se'et of God (13:11; 31:23) "majesty" (Gen 4:7 "a lifting up"); one bracketed note at 13:11 says that it is from the same verb as "lift the face". pachdo (13:11) "the dread of him", like "the terror of him" (9:34). halo governs both halves of 13:11, as at 7:1. seter: basseter "in secret".
- hatov ki (13:9) "Will it be good when"; chaqar "search out", as listed. ha- ... im in 13:9 is put as two questions.
- zikkaron (13:12) "reminder", beside zakar "remember": what the friends have told Job to remember (4:7; 8:8). mashal "proverb". gav (13:12) "defense": the word is the boss of a shield at 15:26 and a mound in Ezek 16:24; the English is left as open, and the repetition is kept ("your defenses are defenses of clay").
- hecherish min (13:13; Jer 38:27) "be silent and let (someone) be", beside chadal min "leave alone" (7:16). avar al of what befalls a person (13:13) "come over" ("pass by" at 9:11): weya'avor alay mah "and let come over me what may".
- al-mah (13:14) "Why", as at 10:2; the imperfects are put in the English present ("Why do I carry"), since Job is doing it. nasa with be- of what holds a thing (13:14) "carry". sim nefesh bekaf (13:14; Judg 12:3; 1 Sam 19:5; 28:21; Ps 119:109) "put one's life in one's hand": the set phrase for risking it, where "palm" would not be English, as at 10:3; kaf stays "palm" in 13:21.
- qatal (13:15; 24:14) "kill": it shares its English with harag. yichel stays "wait" (6:11): lo ayachel "I will not wait", left as open as the Hebrew (he has nothing to wait for, or he will not hold back). akh (13:15) "but"; (13:20) "Only".
- gam-hu (13:16; 31:28) "This too" [alt at 13:16: He too] (decision log, call: close). yeshu'ah "salvation", the same English as soteria, beside hoshia "save"; yesha stays "safety" (5:4, 11). chanef "a godless man", as listed.
- shim'u shamoa (13:17; 21:2) "Hear ... carefully": an infinitive absolute with an imperative, where "certainly" does not fit. achawah (only here) "declaration", so that chiwwah (15:17; 32:6) can be "declare".
- arakh mishpat (13:18; 23:4) "set out one's case": mishpat "case", as planned, with "my" supplied. etsdaq (13:18) "will be found righteous": tsadaq stays "be righteous", and "found" carries the imperfect, the outcome of the case he has set out (the perfect tsadaqti stays "I am righteous").
- mi-hu yariv immadi (13:19) "Who is it that will contend with me?" (compare Isa 50:8). ki-attah with imperfects (13:19) "For then", as at 6:3; "for if I were silent now, I would breathe my last" is not noted.
- asah im (13:20) "do to". nistar mippene "hide from the face of". hirchiq "put far away", as listed (11:14). 13:21b repeats 9:34b, now said to God, and the English repeats it ("let the terror of you not terrify me").
- heshiv with a person, of words (13:22; 33:5, 32) "reply to", kept distinct from anah "answer".
- kammah (13:23) "How many" (7:19 "How long"). awonot, the plural of avon (13:23, 26) "guilty deeds", keeping "guilt". pesha stays "rebellion"; hodia "let (someone) know" (10:2). ne'urim "youth".
- oyev "enemy", as planned; one bracketed note at 13:24 says that it sounds like the name "Job". chashav le- "count as", as listed.
- aleh "leaf"; niddaf "driven". arats (13:25) "terrorize": it stands beside bi'et "terrify" and emah "terror" in this chapter, and the three English words are closer than the three Hebrew ones (the Job 9 line). qash "stubble", leaving "straw" for teven and "chaff" for mots (21:18). radaf "pursue". yavesh (adjective) "dry".
- katav al "write against". merorot (13:26) "bitter things": it shares its English with mamrorim (9:18). horish "make inherit", beside honchal "be made to inherit" (7:3). 13:26-27 is one sentence.
- sad (13:27; 33:11) "stocks". shamar "keep watch on", as the Job 10 line planned. shorshe raglay "the soles of my feet": shoresh is the bottom of a thing (28:9; 36:30); it shares its English with kaf regel. hitchaqqah al (13:27; only here) "draw a line around" [alt: cut a mark into] (decision log, call: close).
- raqav "something rotten" (Hos 5:12, beside "moth"). balah "wear out". beged "garment" (salmah stays "clothes"). akal of a moth "eat".

## Settled in Job 14
- Hebrew text: the Masoretic Text, read by its Ketiv. 14:5: the Ketiv chuqqo "his limit" is followed; the Qere chuqqav "his limits" changes only the number, so nothing is noted. The main text keeps to the Masoretic vowels and letters: 14:3 enekha is singular, "your eye". Not noted: 14:3, where the old Greek and other ancient versions have "him" for "me" (Job is the human being he has been speaking of since 13:28, so the sense is the same); 14:6, the change of weyechdal to a command to God, "and cease", on the pattern of 7:16 and 10:20 (either way God is asked to leave the man in peace); 14:14, where the old Greek has a statement, "he will live", for the question; 14:16, where the old Greek has "nothing of my sins has escaped you" (it is cited in the decision log, on the side of the alt); and what the old Greek puts differently (14:4-5, 12), as in Job 2 to 13.
- Job's fourth speech ends at 14:22: the chapter opens with a quotation mark, as the Job 4 line says, and closes the speech. Job speaks to God all through ("you" is singular), of a human being as "he" and, in 14:3 and 14:13-17, of himself.
- yelud ishshah (14:1; 15:14; 25:4) "born of a woman", set between commas: it describes every human being. qetsar yamim "short of days", beside seva "full of", as planned; rogez stays "turmoil".
- tsits "flower". yatsa (14:2) "come out", as at 1:21 and 5:6. wayyimmal (14:2; the form returns at 18:16; 24:24) "wither", as a flower does in Isa 40:7; the other verb with these letters, "be cut off", is not noted, since the flower's short life ends either way. barach "flee" and tsel "shadow", as listed. amad of what does not last (14:2) "stay" ("stand" at 8:15). The perfect and the wayyiqtols of 14:2 are put in the English present, as at 7:6.
- af at the head of 14:3 "even"; the sentence has no question particle and is put as a question, like 2:9 and 11:3. paqach ayin al "open one's eye on". al-zeh "on such a one". An object set first (we'oti) "is it me ...", as at 13:7-8. hevi bemishpat im "bring into judgment with", as the Job 9 line planned.
- mi yitten with a noun as its object (14:4; compare 31:31) "Who can bring ...?": the question is kept, since "Not one" answers it. As a wish ("If only what is clean could come out of what is unclean!") the line says the same thing, and nothing is noted. tahor "what is clean" and tame "what is unclean", left as open as the Hebrew (a thing or a person). lo echad "Not one".
- charuts (14:5) "determined". chodesh "month": it shares its English with yerach (3:6; 7:3). ittakh "with you". choq (14:5, 13) "limit", the same English both times, so that Job is heard asking for a limit to his time in Sheol like the one set on his life; it shares its English with takhlit (11:7), and elsewhere "portion" or "decree" may fit (23:12, 14; 28:26). chuqqo asita "you have set his limit": asah is "set" where English says it so; shit choq le- (14:13) "set a limit for". avar of a limit (14:5; 19:8) "pass", its object ("it") supplied. 14:5-6 is one sentence; the one im governs all of 14:5 and is repeated in English ("If ..., if ..., if ..."), as it is carried over at 10:15.
- chadal of the man, with nothing after it (14:6) "rest": he is to have a pause from the turmoil of 14:1, as the wicked "have ceased from turmoil" (3:17); "cease" alone would be heard as dying. In 14:7 it stays "cease", and the echo between the two is not shown. we- with a jussive after a command "so that he may", as at 10:20.
- ratsah (14:6) "complete" [alt: enjoy] (decision log, call: close): the verb of paying off what is owed (Lev 26:34, 41, 43; Isa 40:2); elsewhere in Job it will be "accept" or "be pleased with" (33:26; 34:9). ad with an imperfect "until", as the Job 8 line said. sakir "hired worker" and sha'ah min "look away from", as listed.
- ets "tree". nikhrat of a tree "be cut down". hechelif of a tree (14:7) "sprout again", the "again" also carrying od; it shares its English with tsamach, and at 29:20 "be renewed" may fit. chalifah (14:14) "relief", as the Job 10 line planned; one bracketed note at 14:14 says that it is from the same verb as "sprout again" (14:7). It is a note and not an alt: the word itself means a relay or a relief, and the tree is heard only in its root.
- hizqin "grow old", beside zaqen "elder". geza "stump". ba'arets (14:8) "in the earth"; afar stays "dust" (14:8, 19). reach "scent". hifriach "bud". qatsir (14:9; 18:16; 29:19) "branches": not qatsir "harvest" (5:5), which has the same letters; asah qatsir "put out branches". neta "sapling". im (14:8) "Though", as at 9:15; 14:8-9 is one sentence.
- chalash (14:10) "be laid low": the word is for being weak or brought down (Joel 3:10; Isa 14:12). gever "man", adam "a human being" and gava "breathe one's last", as listed. we'ayyo "and where is he?"
- azal "be gone". yam without the article, beside "a river" (14:11), "a sea" (7:12 and 9:8 "the sea"). nahar "river". charav "be drained", beside yavesh "dry up". The "and" that sets a saying beside its picture (14:12, 19) is "so", with "As" before the picture, like "as" at 5:7 and 12:11; 14:11-12 and 14:18-19 are each one sentence.
- ad-bilti shamayim (14:12) "until the heavens are no more", set with what follows it, as the Masoretic accents divide the line. heqits "awake"; ne'or "be roused", beside orer "rouse" (3:8); shenah "sleep" (noun), beside yashan. The plural "they" after "a man" is kept as the Hebrew has it.
- 14:13: mi yitten "If only", tsafan "hide away" and histir "hide", as listed. shuv of anger "turn back", as heshiv is at 9:13. zakar "remember".
- im yamut gever hayichyeh (14:14) "If a man dies, will he live?": no "again" is added. The imperfects of 14:14b-15 are "would", carrying on the wish of 14:13. tsava "a soldier's service" and yichel "wait", as listed.
- 14:15 takes up 13:22 ("call, and I myself will answer"), and the English repeats it. kasaf "yearn for", kept distinct from teshuqah "longing" and sha'af "pant for". ma'aseh yadekha "the work of your hands", as at 1:10.
- 14:16-17: ki-attah after the wish is "For then", as at 6:3 and 13:19, and both verses say what would be; read as "For now", they say what God does now, with 14:16b as a question (decision log, call: ask). One bracket, at the end of 14:17, gives the alt for both verses. tsa'ad "step"; safar "count" (sipper stays "recount"). shamar al "keep watch on", as the Job 10 line planned. chatum "sealed" (chatam be'ad stays "seal up", 9:7); tseror "bag". tafal al (14:17) "plaster over", as listed for 13:4.
- we'ulam (14:18) "But", as listed. naval of a mountain "crumble away" (of a leaf or a flower it will be "fade"). tsur "rock". ataq (qal) "be moved", beside he'tiq "move" (9:5); the line returns at 18:4.
- shachaq "wear away". shataf "wash away". sefiheha (14:19) "its floods": the word is read of water poured out, and "its" of the water. Everywhere else in Scripture the word is what grows of itself (Lev 25:5, 11; 2 Kgs 19:29), which would give "you wash away what grows of itself from the dust of the earth"; this is not noted, since the picture says the same thing (as with "sparks", 5:7). he'evid "destroy", the same English as ibbed (12:23). tiqwah stays "hope" (14:7, 19), so that the tree's hope and the mortal's are heard together.
- taqaf "overpower" (15:24). lanetsach "forever" and halakh of one who dies "go" (10:21), as listed. shinnah "change" (it shares its English with metatithemi); meshanneh fanav "you change his face", the participle made a verb, as at 5:12. shillach "send away", as the Job 8 line planned.
- kaved (14:21) "be honored" (6:3 "be heavier"). tsa'ar "be brought low", beside mits'ar "a small thing" (8:7). banav "his sons", as at 1:5 and 8:4. bin le- "discern", as listed: lamo is put as "it", what becomes of them.
- akh (14:22) "Only", as at 13:20. alav twice in 14:22: "on him" of his flesh and "within him" of his soul, as a soul is said to be "upon" a person in Ps 42:5-6 (the phrase returns at 30:16). Read as "for himself" (the usual word with "mourn": Hos 10:5), the line says the same thing, that he feels only what is his own, so nothing is noted. ka'av "be in pain", beside ke'ev "pain"; aval "mourn" (it shares its English with qoder, 5:11).

## Settled in Job 15
- Hebrew text: the Masoretic Text, read by its Ketiv. The three Qere change only the spelling, so nothing is noted: 15:15 (the written biqdshw is the plural "his holy ones" spelled short, as the plurals of 4:18 and "the holy ones" of 5:1 show), 15:22 (tsafu / tsafuy "watched for") and 15:31 (two spellings of shaw). The main text keeps to the Masoretic vowels and letters, and two readings that differ from them are alts, each with an entry in the decision log: 15:23 ayyeh "Where is it?" [alt: the same letters read as ayyah, a bird of prey, "as food for the vulture", as the old Greek has it] (call: ask); 15:29 minlam (only here) "their gain" [alt: the old Greek "shadow"] (call: close). Not noted: 15:11, la'at read as the verb "cover" of 2 Sam 19:4 ("a word kept hidden with you"), which the form as it stands does not allow; 15:18, the change that makes "their fathers" the subject of "conceal", which no old copy has; 15:30, where the old Greek has "may his blossom fall off" for "he will depart by the wind of his mouth"; 15:32, where the old Greek has "will be destroyed" for timmale; and what the old Greek puts differently (15:20, 24, 28), as in Job 2 to 14.
- Eliphaz's second speech is 15:2-35, all in this chapter. Words quoted inside it take single marks (15:23). He speaks to Job as "you" (singular) to 15:17 and of the wicked man as "he" from 15:20.
- da'at ruach (15:2) "windy knowledge": a noun of quality set after another noun is an adjective, as at 8:6, and ruach stays "wind" (6:26; 8:2), so that Job's answer, "words of wind" (16:3), can be heard. qadim "the east wind". beten of a man's body (15:2) "belly"; in the birth picture of 15:35 it stays "womb" (1:21; 3:10), and one bracketed note there says that the speech opens and closes with the one word. 15:2-3 is one question.
- hokhiach (15:3) "argue", as the Job 13 line says. sakan (15:3; 22:2; 34:9; 35:3) "be of use"; ho'il (15:3; 21:15; 35:3) "gain". Keep the two distinct. davar beside millim (15:3) "talk".
- af-attah (15:4) "You yourself even". hefer with yir'ah (15:4) "do away with": "frustrate" (5:12) does not fit, so the echo is not shown; yir'ah with no object "fear of God", as the Job 4 line planned. gara (15:4, 8) "take away", the same English both times ("take away for yourself" at 15:8); at 36:7 "withdraw" may fit. sichah (15:4; Ps 119:97, 99) "meditation", kept distinct from siach "complaint".
- allef (15:5; 33:33; 35:11) "teach": it shares its English with horah. 15:5a "your guilt teaches your mouth" [alt: your mouth makes your guilt known] (decision log, call: close). leshon arumim "the tongue of the shrewd", arum as listed: Eliphaz is heard using his own word (5:12-13).
- hirshia "condemn", as planned: Eliphaz takes up Job's "my mouth would condemn me" (9:20). anah be- (15:6; 16:8) "testify against" (the same English as martyreo); anah elsewhere stays "answer".
- rishon adam (15:7) "the first human being", adam as at 5:7. cholal (15:7; Prov 8:25) "be brought to birth", kept distinct from nolad "be born"; hitcholel (15:20), from the same root, "writhe"; the echo is not shown. giv'ah "hill".
- sod (15:8) "council" (Jer 23:18, 22; Ps 89:7); at 19:19 and 29:4 another wording will be needed. shama be- "listen in". The imperfects of 15:8 are put in the English present.
- yada and bin (15:9) "know" and "discern": Eliphaz answers 13:1-2 in Job's words. immanu "with us", like immo (12:13, 16).
- sav (15:10) "the gray-haired"; yashish "the aged", as listed. kabbir with yamim "great in days" (kabbir is "mighty" at 8:2): kabbir me'avikha yamim "greater in days than your father".
- me'at min (15:11) "too little for". tanchumot (15:11; 21:2) "comforts", beside nicham and nechamah. la'at "gentle" (Isa 8:6; 2 Sam 18:5): davar la'at immakh "a word that is gentle with you".
- laqach of the heart (15:12) "carry away". razam (only here) "flash". heshiv ruach el (15:13) "turn one's spirit against" (9:18 heshiv ruach "get one's breath back"); ruach of a person's temper "spirit", as at 7:11. hotsi millin "bring out words", the English of 8:10.
- 15:14-16 says again what the voice said at 4:17-19, and the English follows 4:18 where the Hebrew does: hen be- ... lo ya'amin "Look, in ... he puts no trust". zakah (15:14; 25:4) and zakhakh (15:15; 25:5) both "be pure", as the Job 4 line planned. mah enosh ki "What is a mortal, that", as at 7:17. yelud ishshah as a subject "one born of a woman". af ki after a negative (15:16) "how much less", as at 9:14. nit'av "detestable", beside ti'ev "detest"; ne'elach (15:16; Ps 14:3) "corrupt". shatah "drink".
- chiwwah "declare", chazah "gaze on", sipper "recount" and kichad "conceal", as listed and planned; the object of "declare" ("it") is supplied. shema li "hear me". zeh as a relative (15:17) "what". 15:17-19 is one sentence: me'avotam (15:18) is taken with "tell" ("what the wise tell from their fathers"), and 15:19 is set after a dash. zar "stranger". ha'arets (15:19) "the land".
- rasha alone, of one person (15:20) "the wicked man", like chanef (8:13). mispar shanim "the number of years", a length of time like "all his days", with "through" supplied. nitspenu le- "hidden away for", tsafan as planned: with le- the verb is to store up for someone (21:19; Prov 13:22), and "hidden from" has min (24:1), so "the number of years is hidden from the ruthless" is not noted.
- qol pechadim (15:21) "sounds of dread", pachad as listed. bashshalom "in a time of peace" (5:24 "at peace"). shoded "destroyer", as planned; bo with a suffix (15:21; 20:22) "come upon" (it shares its English with atah).
- he'emin with an infinitive (15:22) "believe that", as at 9:16. tsafah (15:22; Ps 37:32) "watch for": tsafuy ele charev "watched for by the sword".
- nadad (15:23) "wander about" (hit'ah stays "make wander"). nakhon "ready", as planned (12:5); beyado "at his hand".
- tsar "distress", as listed; metsuqah "anguish" (it shares its English with chilah, 6:10). A singular verb after the two nouns (15:24) is "they", as at 12:7. taqaf "overpower", as planned. atid "ready", as at 3:8, and hit'atted le- (15:28; only here) "be ready to become", so that the echo is heard. kidor (only here) "attack".
- natah yad el (15:25) "stretch out one's hand against"; natah stays "stretch out" in 15:29. hitgabber el (15:25; 36:9) "act the mighty man against", beside gevurah "might" (gibbor, 16:14, is to be "a mighty man").
- ruts el / al (15:26; 16:14) "run at". betsawwar (15:26) "with a stiff neck": "stiff" is supplied (compare Ps 75:5). avi "thickness": ba'avi gabbe maginnav "with the thick bosses of his shields". gav of a shield "boss", as the Job 13 line said; magen "shield".
- chelev and pimah (15:27; the second only here) both "fat": English has no second plain word. kesel (15:27) "flanks": not kesel "confidence" (8:14), which has the same letters. asah pimah "put on fat".
- nikhchad of cities (15:28) "be wiped out", as at 4:7. yashav "live", as listed; the unnamed plural subject is "no one". gal "stone heap", as planned. The wayyiqtols of 15:27-28 carry on the perfect and tell what the man has done; read as what will become of him ("he will live in cities that are wiped out") they are not noted.
- ashar (15:29) "be rich". qum of wealth (15:29) "last": the Job 8 line planned "stay up", which wealth does not do in English. minleh (only here) "gain"; the plural "their" is kept as the Hebrew has it, as at 5:5.
- From 15:29 the imperfects are "will" (the end of the wicked man); in 15:20-24 they are put in the English present (what he lives with now), and so are 15:34-35.
- sur min (15:30) "depart from" and sur alone "depart", the same English both times in the verse (1:1 "turn away from"). shalhevet "flame"; yibbesh (piel) "dry up"; yoneqet "shoots", as planned. ruach piv "the wind of his mouth", left as open as the Hebrew (God's mouth, as at 4:9, or his own, as at 15:2).
- nit'ah (15:31) "led astray": "wandering" would be heard with "wanders about" (15:23), which is another verb. he'emin be- "put trust in" and shaw "emptiness", as planned. temurah (15:31; 20:18; 28:17) "exchange": temurato "what he gets in exchange".
- belo yomo (15:32) "before his time": the idiom ("when it is not his day") is translated by its meaning (compare 22:16; Eccl 7:17). timmale, said of the exchange, "be paid in full". kippah "frond" (Isa 9:14; 19:15); ra'anan "green".
- chamas (the verb; 15:33) "tear off"; boser "unripe grapes"; gefen "vine"; hishlikh "throw off"; zayit "olive tree"; nitstsah "blossom" (tsits stays "flower"). The article of a comparison (kaggefen, kazzayit) is "a" in English.
- edah (15:34; 16:7) "company". chanef "the godless" and galmud "barren", as listed (8:13; 3:7). shochad "bribery", beside shachad "pay a bribe" (6:22). The perfect akhelah is put in the English present.
- 15:35 takes up 4:8, and amal stays "misery" and awen "harm". The infinitives are made finite verbs ("They conceive ... and give birth to"). hekhin (15:35) "prepare" (hekhin lev stays "set one's heart right"). mirmah "deceit": it shares its English with remiyyah (13:7), a noun from the same root.

## Settled in Job 16
- Hebrew text: the Masoretic Text, read by its Ketiv. The one Qere (16:16, chomarmeru for the written chomarmerah) changes only the number of the verb, so nothing is noted. The main text keeps to the Masoretic vowels, and two readings that differ from them are alts, each with an entry in the decision log: 16:20 melitsay re'ay, both plural, "My friends scoff at me" [alt: the same letters read as singular, with the other sense of melits, "My spokesman is my friend"] (call: ask); 16:21 uven-adam "a human being" [alt: the same letters read as "and between"] (call: close). Not noted: 16:5, where the old Greek has "I would not hold back the movement of my lips"; 16:13, rabbav taken as "his arrows" (the picture is the same); 16:20, where the old Greek has "May my prayer come to the Lord"; and what the old Greek puts differently (16:7, 9-10, 12, 14-16), as in Job 2 to 15.
- Job's fifth speech is 16:2-17:16: chapter 16 ends with no closing quotation mark, as the Job 4 line says. "You" is plural in 16:2 and 16:4-5 (the friends) and singular in 16:3 (Eliphaz); in 16:7b-8 "you" (singular) is God, between the "he" of 16:7a and the "he" of 16:9-14. English does not show the changes, and nothing is added (the Job 7 line).
- ke'elleh rabbot "many things like these" (12:3 kemo-elleh "such things as these"). menachame amal (16:2) "comforters who bring misery": amal stays "misery", so that Job is heard throwing back Eliphaz's last line (15:35), and "who bring" carries the construct; "miserable comforters" would be heard as comforters who are poor at it. kullekhem "all of you", set last with "you are" supplied, as at 13:4.
- qets "end" and divre ruach "words of wind", as planned (15:2). himrits (16:3; the causative of nimrats, 6:25) "pain" (verb): mah-yamritsekha "what pains you".
- gam anokhi (16:4) "I myself also", like gam-ani at 13:2; kakhem "as you do" (12:3). The cohortatives and the imperfect of 16:4-5 are "could": what Job would be able to do in their place. Whether 16:5 is more of the same talk or the real help he once gave (4:3-4) is left as open as the Hebrew, with no "but" added. lu yesh (16:4) "If ... were" (lu as a wish stays "If only", 6:2); the clause goes with what follows it, as the Masoretic accents divide the verse. nafshekhem tachat nafshi "your soul in the place of my soul": nefesh is kept as "soul", singular as in the Hebrew.
- hechbir be- (16:4; only here) "string together": the verb is "join". heni'a rosh al "shake one's head at": in Scripture the gesture is scorn (2 Kgs 19:21; Ps 22:7; 109:25; Lam 2:15); nud le- stays "grieve with" (2:11).
- immets "make firm", as at 4:4: Job takes up what Eliphaz said of him. bemo "with". nid (16:5; only here) "movement". chasakh "restrain" (16:5, 6), as at 7:11; in 16:5 its object ("your pain") is supplied from 16:6. mah minni yahalokh (16:6) "what of it goes from me?": "of it" is supplied; the "if" is carried over to "and if I cease", as at 10:15.
- akh-attah (16:7) "Surely now" (akh is "but" at 13:15 and "Only" at 13:20). hel'ah "weary" (verb), beside la'ah "be weary" (4:2, 5); its subject is not named ("he"; the pain of 16:6 could also be meant, and nothing is noted, since the next words turn to God as "you"). heshem (the causative of shamem) "devastate"; the qal of people (17:8; 18:20; 21:5) is to be "be appalled". edah "company", as planned (15:34): Job answers "the company of the godless is barren".
- qamat (16:8; 22:16) "shrivel up" [alt at 16:8: seize] (decision log, call: close). ed "witness" (10:17). kachash (16:8) "leanness" [alt: the lie told about me] (decision log, call: close); kichesh be- stays "deny" (8:18). qum be- "rise against". anah befanay (16:8; Hos 5:5) "testify to my face": the Job 15 line planned "testify against", but here be- goes with "face".
- taraf "tear", beside teref "prey" (4:11); its object ("me") is supplied from the next verb. satam (16:9; 30:21; Gen 27:41; 50:15) "bear a grudge against", kept distinct from sane "hate" and eivah "hostility"; where it is an attack (30:21; Gen 49:23) another wording may fit. charaq al be-shinnayim "grind one's teeth at" (not "gnash"). tsar "foe", as listed. latash enayim le- "sharpen one's eyes against": the image is kept (the verb is used of a sword, Ps 7:12). The perfects of 16:9-10 are put in the English perfect and the imperfects of 16:9-11 and 16:13-14 in the present; 16:12 is a past scene.
- pa'ar al be-peh (16:10) "gape at ... with the mouth" (29:23); pihem "their mouth", singular as in the Hebrew. cherpah "insult" (19:5; the verb cheref, 27:6), kept distinct from qalon "disgrace", boshet "shame", buz "contempt" and la'ag "mock". hikkah "strike" (2:7); lechi "cheek". hitmalle al (only here) "mass against".
- hisgir el (16:11) "hand over to", as the Job 11 line planned. awil (16:11) "the unjust" [alt: a child] (decision log, call: close): awwal (18:21; 27:7; 29:17; 31:3) is to be "the unjust", beside awlah "injustice"; awilim (19:18; 21:11) "young children". yirteni al-yede (16:11) "hurl into the hands of".
- shalew (16:12) "at ease", the same English as shalah (3:26; 12:6): two forms of one word. pirper and pitspets (16:12; both only here) "break to pieces" and "dash to pieces": the two verbs rhyme in Hebrew, and "to pieces" twice lets that be heard, so no note is needed; katat stays "shatter" and dikka "crush". achaz be- "seize by" (the alt at 16:8 has "seize" too, as 22:16 will need). oref "the back of the neck" (tsawwar stays "neck", 15:26). heqim le- "set up as" ("raise up" at 4:4). mattarah "target" (1 Sam 20:20; Lam 3:12): it shares its English with mifga (7:20).
- savav al "surround". rav (16:13; Jer 50:29) "archer". pillach "split open" (Prov 7:23). kelayot "kidneys": the word is kept, also where it is the seat of feeling (19:27). lo yachmol "does not spare", as listed. mererah (16:13; only here) "gall" (merorah, 20:14, 25). la'arets "on the ground", as at 2:13.
- parats (16:14) "breach" (verb), with its cognate object kept: perets al-pene farets "breach upon breach" ("spread out" stays for herds, 1:10). ruts al "run at" and gibbor "a mighty man", as the Job 15 line planned: Job turns Eliphaz's picture of the wicked man (15:25-26) around. (The Job 10 decision log quotes this line as "like a warrior"; the chapter follows the Job 15 line.)
- saq "sackcloth"; tafar "sew", as in Gen 3:7; geled (only here) "skin": it shares its English with or. olel (16:15; in this sense only here) "thrust": olalti ve'afar qarni "I have thrust my horn into the dust"; qeren "horn", the image kept (1 Sam 2:1; Ps 75:10). The same letters as the verb "deal severely with" (Lam 1:22) are not noted: either way his horn is down in the dust.
- chomarmar of a face (16:16) "be red"; of the inward parts (Lam 1:20; 2:11) another wording will be needed. bekhi "weeping", beside bakah "weep". af'appayim "eyelids" and tsalmawet "the shadow of death", as listed. 16:16-17 is one sentence.
- al lo (16:17) "though there is no", al as the Job 10 line planned. chamas (noun) "violence" (the verb at 15:33 stays "tear off"). kaf stays "palm". tefillah "prayer"; zakh "pure", as listed.
- erets as one spoken to (16:18) "Earth", with no "O". kissah "cover" (9:24); dam "blood". za'aqah "cry" (noun), beside tsa'aq "cry out" (Gen 4:10, which the line calls to mind). al-yehi maqom le- "let there be no resting place for": maqom stays "place", and "resting" is supplied, since "no place for my cry" would be heard as shutting it out.
- gam-attah (16:19) "Even now". sahed (16:19; an Aramaic word, Gen 31:47) "the one who testifies for me", kept distinct from ed "witness". bashamayim "in the heavens"; bammeromim "on high", as marom at 5:11.
- helits with a person as its object (16:20; Ps 119:51) "scoff at", kept distinct from la'ag "mock" and sachaq le- "laugh at"; the participle with "my friends" is made a verb, as at 5:12. melits as a noun (33:23; Gen 42:23) "spokesman". dalaf of an eye (16:20) "shed tears": the verb is "drip" (Eccl 10:18), and "tears" is supplied. eni "my eye", singular as in the Hebrew. 16:20-21 is one sentence.
- hokhiach le- ... im (16:21) "argue for ... with", as the Job 13 line says ("arbiter" stays at 9:33); we- with a jussive after the weeping "that he would". The "he" is left as open as the Hebrew. ben-adam "a human being", as adam is (5:7): "son of" is translated by its meaning, as with bene qedem. The "and" that sets a picture beside a saying is "as ... does" (5:7; 12:11).
- shenot mispar (16:22) "a few years": the idiom ("years of number") is translated by its meaning (Gen 34:30; Num 9:20), so its echo with "the number of years" (15:20) is not shown. atah of years "come". orach lo-ashuv "the path from which I will not return" (10:21).

## Settled in Job 17
- Hebrew text: the Masoretic Text; the chapter has no Qere. The main text keeps to the Masoretic vowels. Three places where the Hebrew can be read in two ways have alts, each with an entry in the decision log: 17:5 "One who informs on friends for a share" [alt: A man invites friends to a share] (call: ask); 17:6 tofet lefanim "one whose face is spat on" [alt: formerly I was like a drum] (call: close); 17:16 nachat "rest" [alt: the same word as "going down", or with other vowels "we go down", as the old Greek has it, and the verse as two questions] (call: close). Not noted: 17:3, orveni read with other vowels as a noun ("Lay down my pledge with yourself"), which asks for the same thing; 17:15, where the old Greek has "my good" for the second "my hope"; 17:16, where the old Greek has "with me" for "the bars of"; and what the old Greek puts differently (17:1-3, 5-7, 11), as in Job 2 to 16.
- Job's fifth speech ends at 17:16: the chapter opens with a quotation mark, as the Job 4 line says, and closes the speech. "You" is singular in 17:3-4 (God) and plural in 17:10 (the friends); God is "he" in 17:6, with no name, as at 16:7, and the friends are "their" and "they" in 17:2, 4 and 12. English does not show the changes, and nothing is added (the Job 7 line). Words quoted inside the speech take single marks (17:12, 14).
- chubbal (17:1; Isa 10:27) "be ruined": the verb of ruining a vineyard or a land (Song 2:15; Isa 13:5). ruach (17:1) "spirit", as at 10:12; "my breath" (19:17) is not noted, since either way it is the life in him that is ruined. niz'akh (only here) "be snuffed out": another form of nid'akh (6:17; 18:5-6), with the same English. qevarim li "graves are mine": the plural is kept (21:32).
- im-lo at the head of a statement (17:2) "Surely"; in an oath (1:11) it stays "certainly". hatulim (only here) "mockers": the noun of hetel "fool" (13:9), with the wording of its own that the Job 13 line asked for; it shares its English with la'ag "mock". immadi (17:2) "with me". hamrot (17:2) "provocation": the verb of Ps 78:17, 40; it shares its English with hirgiz "provoke" (12:6). lin of an eye (17:2) "dwell on"; of people and animals it is to be "spend the night" (24:7; 31:32; 39:9), and of what stays with someone "stay" may fit (19:4; 41:22). (The Job 16 decision log quotes this line as "my eye rests on their provocation"; the chapter says "dwells", so that "rest" is kept for nuach and nachat, 17:16.)
- sim with no object (17:3) "lay down a pledge": "a pledge" is supplied, as "lots" is at 6:27; the noun for a thing left as security is made from this verb (Lev 6:2), and the two clauses that follow are about a pledge. Taken as short for "set your heart" (mesim, 4:20; 23:6), "Give heed now", it is not noted, since either way what is asked is that God be his guarantor. na "now", as listed. arav with a person as its object (17:3; Gen 43:9; Ps 119:122; Isa 38:14) "be guarantor for": orveni "be my guarantor" ("surety" is no longer plain English). immakh "with yourself". nitqa leyad (17:3; only here) "clasp (someone's) hand in pledge": the idiom of striking hands (Prov 6:1; 17:18; 22:26) is translated by its meaning, with the hand kept. mi hu "Who is it that", as at 13:19.
- tsafan min (17:4) "hide away from", as planned (10:13). sekhel "insight", beside haskil "gain insight" (Gen 3:6); libbam "their heart", singular as in the Hebrew. al-ken "that is why", as listed. romem (17:4) "raise high", beside marom "on high" (5:11; 16:19); its object ("them") is supplied, as at 5:4.
- higgid with a person as its object (17:5) "inform on" [alt: invite] (decision log, call: ask); elsewhere it stays "tell". chelek "share"; where it is what God allots (20:29; 27:13; 31:2) "portion" may fit better. re'im "friends", with no "his". kalah of eyes "fail" and banav "his sons", as planned (11:20; 14:21). The verse has no "one who": a clause without asher (3:3) that is picked up by "his" takes a dash, as at 3:4, and the imperfect is "will fail".
- hitstsig (17:6) "set (as)": it shares its English with sim (7:20); heqim le- stays "set up as" (16:12). meshol (17:6; only here in this form) "byword", beside mashal "proverb" (13:12); millah in the same sense (30:9) will share it. ammim "the peoples", the article supplied as at 12:2.
- tofet lefanim ehyeh (17:6) "I am one whose face is spat on" [alt: formerly I was like a drum] (decision log, call: close). The name Topheth, which is written the same way, is not noted in the chapter: a Topheth before people is a thing abhorred, which says the same thing.
- kahah (17:7) "grow dim". ka'as (17:7) "grief", as the Job 5 line allowed: it is what wastes an eye (Ps 6:7; 31:9); it stays "resentment" at 5:2; 6:2; 10:17. yetsurim (only here) "limbs": what has been formed. tsel "shadow", as listed. kullam is set with "my limbs" ("all my limbs").
- shamam al (17:8) "be appalled at", as planned (16:7). yesharim "the upright". naqi, chanef and tsaddiq alone, of one person (17:8-9) "the innocent man", "the godless man", "the righteous man", like tam (8:20) and rasha (15:20). hit'orer al (17:8) "rouse oneself against", beside orer "rouse" (3:8) and he'ir "rouse himself" (8:6); at 31:29 another wording will be needed. The imperfects of 17:8-9 are put in the English present.
- achaz with a way or a step as its object (17:9; 23:11) "hold to"; achaz be- stays "seize by" (16:12) and hecheziq be- "hold fast to". tehor-yadayim "the one with clean hands": tahor "clean" (14:4), and yad "hand", leaving "palm" for kaf (9:30). hosif omets (17:9; omets only here) "grow firmer": the noun of immets "make firm" (4:4; 16:5), so that Job is heard taking up his own word.
- we'ulam "But", as listed. kullam with a second-person verb (17:10; 1 Kgs 22:28; Mic 1:2) "all of you". tashuvu uvo'u na (17:10) "come again now": shuv with a second verb is "again", as at 10:16; where it stands alone (6:29) it stays "turn back". welo emtsa "and I will not find": the "and" is kept. chakham "a wise man".
- avar of days (17:11) "pass". zimmah of a person's own plans (17:11) "purpose", leaving "plan" for machshavah (5:12); where it is a shameful act (31:11) another wording will be needed. nittaq (17:11) "be broken off": the verb of a cord that snaps (Judg 16:9; Eccl 4:12); at 18:14 "be torn from" may fit. morash (17:11) "desire": the word is read with areshet "request", which stands beside "the desire of his heart" in Ps 21:2. The same letters are "possession" (Isa 14:23; Obad 17); this is not noted, since what a heart has as its own and what it desires say the same thing here. morashe levavi stays after its clause, as the Masoretic accents divide the line.
- sim X le-Y (17:12) "turn X into Y": the English leaves open, as the Hebrew does, whether night is made day or is put for day (Isa 5:20). or qarov "Light is near": the words are read as what "they" say and stand in single marks, with nothing added, as at 15:23. qarov is "near", as everywhere in Scripture; "the light is short because of darkness" is not noted. mippene (17:12) "in the face of".
- 17:13-15 is one sentence, kept whole across the verse breaks: the one im governs 17:13-14 and is repeated in English ("If ..., if ..., if ..."), as at 14:5, and we- with efo opens the main clause, "where then" (9:24). The perfects under it are "if I have". Read with a break after the first verb ("If I hope, Sheol is my house"), the lines are statements; this is not noted, since either way Sheol is the only house he can hope for, and the question of 17:15 follows. qiwwah "hope for", as listed, so that it is heard with tiqwah "hope" (17:15); with a second object "as".
- rippad (17:13; 41:30) "spread". yatsua (17:13) "bed": it shares its English with mishkav (7:13; eres stays "couch"); the plural is put as "my bed". bachoshekh "in the darkness".
- qara le- with the words said (17:14) "call to", as at 12:4. shachat "the pit", as planned (9:31); the same letters as a noun of the verb "ruin" ("decay") are not noted, since either way it is the grave that he calls his father. av "father", em "mother", achot "sister" (1:4). rimmah as one spoken to (17:14) "the maggot", singular (7:5 "maggots"). The verb is not repeated for the maggot in the Hebrew, and English adds "and".
- ayyeh "where is" (15:23). shur "catch sight of", as listed (7:8): mi yeshurennah "who will catch sight of it?"; the noun set first takes a dash, as at 3:4.
- bad (17:16) "bar": badde she'ol "the bars of Sheol", with "to" supplied, as with a place one goes down to; it is not baddim "idle talk" (11:3), which has the same letters. teradnah, a feminine plural, has no subject but "my hope", named twice in 17:15, and is put as "It will go down". im before a clause with no verb (17:16) "when" (7:4). nachat (17:16) "rest" (noun), beside nuach (3:13, 17) [alt: go down] (decision log, call: close). yachad "together"; al-afar "on the dust" (20:11; 21:26), beside le'afar "in the dust" (7:21).

## Settled in Job 18
- Hebrew text: the Masoretic Text; the chapter has no Qere. The main text keeps to the Masoretic vowels and letters. Five places where the Hebrew can be read in two ways have alts, each with an entry in the decision log: 18:2 qintse (only here) "an end" [alt: snares] (call: close); 18:3 nitminu (the form only here) "unclean" [alt: stupid] (call: ask); 18:12 ono "his vigor" [alt: his harm, the same letters and vowels] (call: clear); 18:14 mivtacho as the thing torn from his tent [alt: he is torn from the tent he trusts in] (call: close); 18:20 acharonim and qadmonim "those in the west" and "those in the east" [alt: those who come after, those who came before] (call: close). Not noted: 18:3, where the old Greek has "we have been silent"; 18:9, where the old Greek has "the thirsty" for tsammim, as at 5:5 (between "trap" and "rope" the sentence does not allow it, as the Job 5 decision log says); 18:12, tsal'o taken as "his side" ("calamity is ready at his side"), since either way calamity is waiting for him; 18:13, the change to "his skin is eaten by disease", and 18:15, the change of mibbeli-lo to a word for fire, which no old copy has; and what the old Greek puts differently (18:4, 7, 9, 11-15, 17, 19), as in Job 2 to 17.
- Bildad's second speech is 18:2-21, all in this chapter. "You" is plural in 18:2-3 (tesimun, tavinu, "your eyes"), though Bildad is answering Job, and singular in 18:4 ("for your sake"); the old Greek has the singular throughout. English does not show the change, and nothing is added (the Job 7 line). The wicked are plural once (18:5 "the light of the wicked") and then "he", as the Hebrew has it. The perfects and imperfects of 18:5-21 are put in the English present, as at 15:20-24.
- ad-anah "How long", as planned (8:2). sim qets le- (18:2; 28:3) "put an end to": the plural qintse is "an end", and "before" is supplied ("How long before you put an end to words?"). bin with no object (18:2) "understand", beside tevunah "understanding" (12:12); it shares its English with epistamai, and bin le- stays "discern". The imperfect tavinu is a command. achar "afterward".
- maddua "Why", carried over to the second half of 18:3 ("and why are we"), as ha- is at 8:11. nechshav ke- "be counted as", like chashav le- (13:24). behemah as a collective (18:3) "beasts", as planned (12:7): Bildad answers "ask the beasts now". nitmah (18:3) "be unclean", the same English as tame (14:4).
- 18:4: taraf "tear" and af "anger", as at 16:9, so that Bildad is heard turning Job's "His anger has torn me" around; nafsho "himself" (9:21 "myself"). The third person is kept and set before a dash, as at 3:4 and 12:4. lema'an with a suffix "for (your) sake". azav stays "abandon". weye'taq-tsur mimmeqomo repeats 14:18 word for word, and the English repeats it ("a rock ... moved from its place").
- gam at the head of a statement (18:5) "Yes". nid'akh "be snuffed out", as planned (6:17): Bildad takes up "my days are snuffed out" (17:1). nagah (18:5; 22:28) "shine": it shares its English with hofia (3:4). sheviv (only here in Hebrew; in the Aramaic of Dan 3:22; 7:9) "flame": it shares its English with shalhevet (15:30). esh "fire". ner "lamp" (the same English as lychnos); alav (18:6) "above him" (29:3). chashakh "go dark" (3:9).
- on (18:7, 12; 40:16) "vigor": tsa'ade ono "his vigorous steps", a noun of quality made an adjective, as at 8:6; it shares its English with kelach "full vigor" (5:26). Where it is what a man has gained (20:10) "wealth" may fit. tsarar of steps (18:7; Prov 4:12) "be cramped". hishlikh with a person as its object (18:7) "throw down" (15:33 "throw off"); atsato "his counsel", with no "own" added, as at 9:20.
- Six words for traps in 18:8-10, kept distinct: reshet "net"; sevakhah "mesh" (in 2 Kgs 1:2 a lattice); pach "trap"; tsammim "snare", as planned (5:5), with no alt here; chevel "rope" (36:8; yeter stays "cord"); malkodet (only here) "what will catch him", so that it is heard with lakad "catch" (5:13; 36:8) and English needs no second "trap". shullach (the passive of shillach) "be sent"; beraglav "by his feet" (Judg 5:15), set first in English so that it is not heard with "net". hithallekh "walk about", as listed. achaz be- "seize by", as at 16:12, its object ("him") supplied; aqev "heel". hecheziq al (18:9) "hold fast", like hecheziq be- "hold fast to". tamun "hidden", as at 3:16; chavlo "the rope for him". nativ (18:10; 19:8; 24:13; 28:7) "pathway", leaving "path" for orach and "way" for derekh.
- ballahot (18:11, 14; 24:17; 27:20; 30:15) "terrors": it shares its English with bi'utim (6:4), and with bi'et "terrify" beside it (18:11) the English words are closer than the Hebrew ones, which only begin alike (the Job 9 line). melekh ballahot "the king of terrors", lowercase. saviv "on every side". hefits with a person as its object (18:11) "drive on"; elsewhere "scatter" may fit (37:11; 38:24). leraglav (18:11) "at his heels": the idiom ("at his feet", of what follows close behind: 1 Sam 25:42; Hab 3:5) is translated by its meaning, so its echo with "by his feet" (18:8) is not shown.
- ra'ev "hungry", as listed (5:5): yehi-ra'ev "goes hungry", the jussive form read as a statement, like yachazeq (18:9). ed "calamity" and nakhon le- "ready for", as planned (12:5). tsela (18:12; Ps 35:15; 38:17; Jer 20:10) "stumbling": it shares its English with kashal (4:4).
- bad of a body (18:13) "limb": badde oro "his skin, limb by limb", baddav "his limbs"; it shares its English with yetsurim (17:7). It is not baddim "idle talk" (11:3) or bad "bar" (17:16), which have the same letters. akal of a sickness "eat", as of a moth (13:28). The first "eats" has no subject named and is "It"; the second names it. bekhor mawet "the firstborn of death", lowercase.
- nittaq min (18:14) "be torn from", as the Job 17 line planned. mivtach "trust" (8:14): mivtacho "what he trusts in". hits'id (only here) "march": the verb of tsa'ad "step" (18:7), and the echo is not shown; its unnamed feminine subject is put in the passive, as at 8:18.
- mibbeli-lo (18:15) "what is not his", like lo-lo (Hab 1:6; 2:6): it is read as the subject of the feminine tishkon, set last as Bildad sets his subjects in these lines (18:9, 13, 15b). Taken with the terrors as the ones who live there (a feminine singular verb goes with ballahot at 27:20), or as "nothing of his lives in his tent", the line still says that his tent has passed from him, so nothing is noted. shakan "live", as listed (4:19). zorah (the passive of zarah) "be scattered": it shares its English with hitpared (4:11). naweh "home", as planned (5:3). gofrit "sulfur" (not "brimstone").
- mittachat and mimma'al (18:16) "below" and "above". yavesh of roots "dry up", yimmal "wither" and qatsir "branches", as planned (8:12; 14:2, 9).
- zekher "memory" (zikhro "his memory"), beside zakar "remember"; zikkaron stays "reminder" (13:12). avad "perish", as listed. shem "name". al-pene chuts (18:17) "on the face of the open country", as chutsot at 5:10, where it also stands beside erets "the earth".
- hadaf "push"; henid (18:18; the causative of nadad) "chase out", kept distinct from garash "drive out" and niddach "be driven away". The unnamed plural subject is put in the passive, as at 4:19. tevel "world": it shares its English with kosmos.
- nin and nekhed (18:19; Gen 21:23; Isa 14:22, always together) "progeny" and "posterity": the two begin alike in Hebrew, and they are kept distinct from zera "offspring" and tse'etsa'im "descendants". sarid "survivor" (20:21, 26; 27:15). megurim "the places where he stayed": gur is to live somewhere as an outsider, kept distinct from yashav and shakan "live".
- al-yomo (18:20) "at his day", left as the Hebrew has it (1:4; 3:1). shamam al "be appalled at", as planned (17:8). achaz sa'ar "be seized with horror": the Hebrew says that they seize it, as shuddering seizes the flesh at 21:6; sa'ar (18:20; Ezek 27:35; 32:10) "horror" shares its English with chatat (6:21).
- akh (18:21) "Surely", as at 16:7. mishkan (18:21; 21:28; 39:6) "dwelling", beside shakan "live" (the echo is not shown); it shares its English with oiketerion. awwal alone, of one person (18:21) "the unjust man", like chanef (17:8); the Job 16 line planned "the unjust", and the singular is kept beside "one who does not know God". meqom lo-yada-el "the place of one who does not know God": a clause without asher, as at 3:3; El "God" (5:8).

## Settled in Job 19
- Hebrew text: the Masoretic Text, read by its Ketiv. The one Qere (19:29, shaddun for the written shaddin) changes only the spelling of "judgment", so nothing is noted. The main text keeps to the Masoretic vowels and letters: 19:13 hirchiq is singular, "He has put ... far away" (the old Greek has the brothers going away of themselves); 19:28 bi "in me" (many Hebrew copies and the old Greek and Latin versions have "in him"; not noted, since the friends' words are reported either way and the sense is the same, as at 14:3). Six places where the Hebrew can be read in two ways have alts, each with an entry in the decision log: 19:4 we'af-omnam shagiti "And even if I have truly gone astray" [alt: And truly I have gone astray] (call: close); 19:17 wechannoti (in this sense only here) "I am repulsive" [alt: I plead] (call: close); 19:25 acharon "at the last" [alt: the Last] (call: close); 19:26 mibbesari "from my flesh" [alt: without my flesh] (call: ask); 19:27 welo-zar "and not a stranger" [alt: and not as a stranger] (call: clear); 19:29 chemah avonot cherev "wrath brings the punishments of the sword" [alt: wrath is guilt that the sword punishes] (call: close). Not noted: 19:3 tahkeru (only here) taken from the verb "be a stranger" ("you make yourselves strangers to me"), since either way they are mistreating him; 19:6 metsudo taken as "his siegeworks" (Eccl 9:14), since either way God has shut him in; 19:17 zarah taken as a second verb, "be loathsome", which stands nowhere else (either way his wife keeps away from his breath), and bene vitni as "my own sons", which the story does not allow (1:18-19; 8:4); 19:24 la'ad read with other vowels as "as a witness", since either way the words are to outlast him; 19:26, where the Latin version has "I will be clothed again with my skin"; 19:29 the change of shaddin to the name "the Almighty", which no old copy has; and what the old Greek puts differently (19:3-4, 17-18, 20, 24-27, 29), as in Job 2 to 18.
- Job's sixth speech is 19:2-29, all in this chapter. "You" is plural all through (the friends), and God is "he" from 19:6. Words quoted inside it take single marks (19:7, 28); in 19:28 the friends' words are given first as a quotation and then as a report ("and that the root of the matter is found in me"), which is how the Masoretic "in me" is read.
- hogah (19:2; Lam 1:5, 12; Isa 51:23) "torment"; nefesh as the seat of feeling "soul" (3:20). ad-anah "How long", dikka "crush" and millim "words", as listed: Job answers Bildad's "How long ... words" (18:2). zeh eser pe'amim (19:3; Num 14:22) "Ten times now". hikhlim "put to shame" and bosh "be ashamed", as planned (11:3; 6:20). hakar (only here) "deal harshly with": the sense is drawn from the line; lo tevoshu tahkeru-li "you are not ashamed to deal harshly with me".
- we'af-omnam with a perfect (19:4) "And even if ... truly" (34:12 will be "Truly"); im-omnam (19:5) "If ... truly"; omnam "truly", as at 9:2. shagah "go astray", as listed (6:24); meshugah (only here) "error": the noun of shagah. It shares its English with tohalah (4:18), and its echo with "go astray" is not shown (as with plane "error" beside planao, 1 Jn 4:6). lin of what stays with someone "stay", as the Job 17 line planned.
- higdil al (19:5; Ps 35:26; 38:16; 55:12) "make oneself great against", beside gadal "be great" (2:13). hokhiach with al and an object (19:5) "argue ... against", as the Job 13 line says. cherpah "insult", as planned (16:10): cherpati "the insult I bear" (compare Ps 69:7), the insult done to him and not one he gives. 19:5-6 is one sentence; efo "then" (9:24; 17:15; 19:6, 23).
- iwwet with a person as its object (19:6; Ps 119:78; Lam 3:36) "wrong" (verb), the other wording that the Job 8 line asked for: Job answers Bildad's "Does God pervert justice?" (8:3) with Bildad's verb, and the echo is not shown. It shares "wrong" with tiflah "wrongdoing" (1:22). metsud (19:6) "hunting net": the noun of tsud "hunt" (10:16), kept distinct from reshet "net" and the other trap words of 18:8-10, which Job is answering. hiqqif al (19:6; Ps 88:17) "close around" (1:5 "run their course").
- tsa'aq with the word that is cried (19:7; compare Hab 1:2; Jer 20:8) "cry out, 'Violence!'"; ne'enah "be answered"; shiwwa (19:7; 24:12; 29:12; 30:20, 28; 35:9; 36:13; 38:41) "cry for help" (it shares "cry" with tsa'aq "cry out" and za'aqah "cry"); mishpat (19:7) "justice", as at 8:3.
- gadar (19:8; Lam 3:7, 9; Hos 2:6) "wall up" (suk be'ad stays "put a hedge around"). orach "path", avar "pass" and nativ "pathway", as planned (14:5; 18:10). The perfects of 19:8-9 are put in the English perfect and the imperfects and wayyiqtols of 19:8-12 in the present, as in chapter 16.
- kavod "glory" (the same English as doxa). hifshit (19:9; 22:6) "strip": it shares its English with sholal "stripped" (12:17); pashat al stays "make a raid on" (1:17). hesir "remove", as listed. atarah (19:9; 31:36) "crown": ateret roshi "the crown from my head", so that it is not heard as qodqod "the crown of the head" (2:7).
- natats (19:10; only here in Job) "break down", kept distinct from haras "tear down" (12:14) and parats "breach" (16:14). saviv "on every side" (19:10, 12), the same English both times; saviv le- (19:12) "on every side of". (The Job 10 decision log quotes 19:12 with "camp around my tent".) halakh of one who is perishing (19:10) "be gone", as at 7:9. hissia (19:10) "pull up", the same English as nissa (4:21). ka'ets tiqwati "my hope like a tree": Job takes back "there is hope for a tree" (14:7).
- hecherah af al (19:11; the causative with af only here) "make one's anger burn against": charah of anger "burn" (42:7); charah le- stays "be angry" (Gen 4:5). chashav ke- (19:11) "count as one of": the plural "his foes" needs "one of" in English; lo is not shown, as lakh is not at 13:24. tsar "foe", as listed.
- gedud (19:12; 25:3; 29:25) "troops": it shares its English with tsava (10:17). salal (19:12; 30:12) "build up": wayyasollu alay darkam "they build up their way against me", derekh staying "way". chanah "camp".
- yode'ay (19:13) "those who know me"; meyudda'ay (19:14; Ps 31:11; 88:8) "those I know well". Keep the two distinct. akh with a verb (19:13) "nothing but". zur (19:13) "become a stranger" and zar "stranger" (19:13, 15, 17, 27; 15:19), one English word all through the chapter, so that the thread is heard; zarah le- (19:17) "is strange to". nokhri "foreigner".
- chadal of people (19:14) "fall away": they have ceased to come (Judg 5:6-7); "cease" alone would be heard as dying, as at 14:6. qarov "near" (17:12): qerovay "those near to me", so that it is heard against "put far away" (19:13).
- gare veti (19:15) "those who stay in my house", gur as at 18:19. amah (19:15; 31:13) "female servant", beside eved "servant". The feminine verb takes both subjects. chashav le- "count as", as listed.
- The perfects of 19:16-19 that tell how things now stand are put in the English present ("I call", "reject", "detest", "those I love"), as ma'asti is at 7:16. qara le- "call to" (12:4). bemo-fi "with my mouth" (16:5). hitchannen le- "plead with", as planned (8:5).
- ruach (19:17) "breath", as the Job 17 line said. bene vitni (19:17) "the sons of my mother's womb": bitni as at 3:10, and they are his brothers, as "my mother's sons" stand beside "my brothers" in Ps 69:8. chanan in the second sense that 19:17 has (only here) "be repulsive", kept apart from naqat "loathe" (10:1) and ti'ev "detest".
- awilim "young children", ma'as be- "reject", ti'ev "detest" and metim "men", as planned (16:11; 5:17; 9:31; 11:3). qum "rise", as listed: aqumah "I rise". dibber be- (19:18; Num 12:1; Ps 50:20) "speak against".
- sod of a person's close friends (19:19) "inner circle", the other wording that the Job 15 line asked for: mete sodi "the men of my inner circle". zeh as a relative (15:17) with a plural verb: "those". ahav "love" (the same English as agapao). nehpakh be- (19:19) "turn against" (hafakh stays "overturn").
- davaq be- "cling to", as in Gen 2:24. atsmi "my bone", ori "my skin" and besari "my flesh", singular as in the Hebrew, as at 2:5: with "the hand of God has touched me" (19:21) Job says, without knowing it, what the adversary asked for (1:11; 2:5), and naga be- stays "touch". hitmallet (19:20; 41:19) "escape", the same English as nimlat (1:15). be'or shinnay "with the skin of my teeth", left as the Hebrew has it.
- chanan (the simple form; 19:21; 33:24) "have pity on"; its echo with hitchannen "plead with" (19:16) is not shown. attem re'ay "you my friends". radaf "pursue", as listed. kemo-El "as God does". sava min (19:22; 31:31) "have one's fill of", as listed (7:4): the image of flesh eaten is kept.
- mi-yitten efo (19:23) "If only, then,". katav "write"; sefer "book", as listed; chaqaq in the passive "be inscribed" (hitchaqqah stays "draw a line around", 13:27). et (19:24) "pen"; barzel "iron", as listed; oferet "lead". la'ad "forever": it shares its English with lanetsach and le'olam. tsur "rock"; chatsav "cut". 19:23-24 is one sentence.
- wa'ani yada'ti (19:25) "And I myself know". go'el "redeemer", lowercase like "savior" and "maker": the word that the Job 3 line kept "redeem" for. It is the next of kin who buys back what the family has lost (Lev 25:25; Ruth 4:4-6), takes up the cause of one who has been wronged (Prov 23:11; Jer 50:34) and answers for his blood (Num 35:19). English has no one word for all of this, and "avenger" or "defender" would each give only a part, so nothing is noted. Who the redeemer is the Hebrew does not say, and nothing is added. chay "lives".
- acharon (19:25) "at the last" [alt: the Last] (decision log, call: close); 18:20 stays as it is. al-afar "on the dust", as at 17:16: left as open as the Hebrew (the ground, as at 41:33, or the dust where the dead lie, 20:11; 21:26). qum "rise", as listed, the verb of 14:12 and 19:18; in a court it is what a witness does (Deut 19:15-16; Ps 27:12).
- 19:26: achar before a perfect "after", as in 42:7. niqqefu (19:26; Isa 10:34) "be struck off": an unnamed plural subject is put in the passive, as at 4:19; kept distinct from hifshit "strip" (19:9). zot is read with "my skin", "this skin of mine", though the two do not agree in gender: he points at the skin he has (7:5; 30:30). mibbesari "from my flesh" [alt: without my flesh] (decision log, call: ask). chazah "gaze on", as planned (8:17), and ra'ah "see": the two verbs of 19:26-27 are kept apart.
- 19:27: asher ani echezeh-li "whom I myself will gaze on for myself": li as lakh is at 5:27; "whom I will see on my side" (li as in Ps 118:6) is not noted, since the word stands joined to the verb. ra'u, a perfect among futures, "will see", as at 5:20, with no object added. welo-zar "and not a stranger" [alt: and not as a stranger] (decision log, call: clear). kalah of kidneys (19:27) "fail", as of eyes (11:20; 17:5); kelayot "kidneys", as the Job 16 line planned. becheqi (19:27) "within me": the idiom ("in my bosom") is translated by its meaning.
- ki tomeru (19:28) "If you say", with 19:29 as its main clause. mah-nirdof-lo "How will we pursue him?": lo marks the object. shoresh davar "the root of the matter": shoresh "root", as listed. nimtsa be- "be found in".
- gur mippene (19:29; Deut 1:17; Num 22:3) "be afraid of": it shares its English with yare min (5:21); yagor stays "shrink from" (3:25). lakhem "yourselves". chemah (19:29; 21:20) "wrath" (6:4 "poison"), leaving "anger" for af and "resentment" for ka'as. avonot (19:29) "punishments" [alt: guilt] (decision log, call: close); elsewhere the plural stays "guilty deeds" (13:23, 26). lema'an with an imperfect "so that". din (19:29; 35:14; 36:17) "judgment": shaddin "that there is a judgment"; mishpat stays "justice" or "case".

## Settled in Job 20
- Hebrew text: the Masoretic Text, read by its Ketiv. The one Qere (20:11, alumav for the written alumo) changes only the spelling of "his youth", so nothing is noted. The main text keeps to the Masoretic vowels and letters, and two readings that differ from them are alts, each with an entry in the decision log: 20:10 yeratstsu "will appease" [alt: the same letters read as the verb "crush", with the poor as its subject, as the old Greek has it] (call: close); 20:22 amel "one in misery" [alt: the same letters read as amal "misery", as the old Greek and Latin have it] (call: close). Three places where the Hebrew can be read in two ways have alts, each with an entry in the decision log: 20:19 welo yivnehu "and he will not build it up" [alt: that he did not build] (call: close); 20:20 bachamudo lo yemallet "he will not escape with what he desires" [alt: he lets nothing that he desires escape] (call: close); 20:23 bilchumo "as his food" [alt: into his flesh] (call: ask). Not noted: 20:2 chushi taken from a second verb, "feel", which stands nowhere else in Scripture unless at Eccl 2:25 (either way it is what is in him that makes him answer); 20:4, where the old Greek has "Do you not know"; 20:9 teshurennu, a feminine verb, taken with "eye" ("and it will no longer catch sight of him in his place"), since either way he is gone from his place, as at 7:10; 20:11, the same letters as "what is hidden" (Ps 90:8), which the feminine verb "lie down" does not go with; 20:18 kechel temurato taken with what follows it ("he will have no enjoyment to match the wealth he got in exchange"), since either way he gets no good of it; 20:21 le'okhlo taken as "nothing is left for him to eat", which "that is why" does not allow; 20:23, where the Latin has "his war" for lechumo; 20:25, where the old Greek has "arrow" for shalaf (the picture is the same), and gewah taken as "body"; 20:26 yera taken from the verbs "graze" or "break", since either way the survivor comes to harm; 20:28 yevul read as "flood" and yigel as "roll away", which no old copy has; and what the old Greek puts differently (20:2, 5-7, 14-15, 18, 26, 28-29), as in Job 2 to 19.
- Zophar's second speech is 20:2-29, all in this chapter. Words quoted inside it take single marks (20:7). The wicked are plural once (20:5 "the wicked") and then "he", as in chapter 18. God is named at 20:15 and 20:29; in 20:23 and 20:28 he is "he" and "his", with no name, and the English leaves it so (the Job 16 line).
- Tenses: the imperfects that tell the man's end are "will", as from 15:29; the perfects of what he has done are in the English perfect (20:15, 19, 20), and those that say how things stand in the present (20:11, 14). 20:25 shalaf wayyetse is put in the present. A participle that carries the description on (20:18 meshiv "He gives back"; 20:27 mitqomamah "will rise up") is made a verb, as at 5:12. Jussive forms with no al (20:23 yehi; 20:26 yera; 20:28 yigel) are read as statements, as at 18:9, 12; al with a jussive (20:17) stays "Let him not", as at 15:31.
- lakhen "Therefore" and se'ippim "anxious thoughts", as listed (4:13). heshiv with a person (20:2) "reply to", as at 13:22: his thoughts give him his answer, as "a spirit ... answers me" (20:3). chush (20:2) "haste": uva'avur chushi vi "and this is because of my haste within me", with "this is" supplied.
- musar (20:3) "discipline", as at 5:17. kelimmah "shame": musar kelimmati "discipline that puts me to shame", so that it is heard with hikhlim "put to shame" (11:3; 19:3). binah "understanding" and ruach of a person "spirit", as planned (11:12; 7:11); min (20:3) "from", left as open as the Hebrew.
- hazot yada'ta (20:4) "Do you know this", with no "not". minni-ad "from of old" (la'ad stays "forever", 19:24). adam with no article, of the race at its beginning (20:4) "humankind", as in Gen 1:26; adam rasha (20:29; 27:13) "a wicked human being". sim "set". 20:4-5 is one sentence, and so are 20:6-7 and 20:12-14.
- renanah "cry of joy", as listed (3:7). miqqarov (20:5) "short": the word is "from near", said of what has only lately come (Deut 32:17). simchah "joy", beside samach "rejoice" and kept distinct from masos "gladness" (8:19). ade-raga "for a moment" (rega "moment", 7:18). chanef "the godless man", as listed.
- si (20:6; only here) "height": it shares its English with govah (11:8). av (20:6; 22:14; 26:8; 30:15; 36:29; 37:11, 16; 38:34) "cloud", "the clouds": it shares its English with anan, and where the two stand together (26:8) "thick clouds" may fit. higgia le- (20:6) "reach", as in Gen 28:12; naga stays "touch".
- gelal "dung". ro'av "those who saw him". ayyo "Where is he?", as at 14:10. uf "fly away" (5:7 "fly"). yuddad (the passive of nadad) "be chased away", beside henid "chase out" (18:18). chezyon laylah "a vision of the night", as at 4:13.
