- shazaf (20:9; 28:7) "glimpse", kept distinct from shur "catch sight of", ra'ah "see" and chazah "gaze on". hosif with no second verb "do so again". 20:9 answers 7:8-10 in Job's words.
- ritstsah (the piel of ratsah; only here) "appease"; chillah fene stays "seek the favor of" (11:19), so that Zophar's two speeches are not made to echo where the Hebrew does not. dal "the poor", as listed. heshiv with a thing as its object (20:10, 18) "give back". on of what a man has gained (20:10) "riches", leaving "wealth" for chayil (20:15, 18), as the Job 18 line allowed.
- alumim (20:11; 33:25) "youth". male "be full of". al-afar "on the dust", as planned (17:16).
- himtiq (20:12) "be sweet". ra'ah as a thing a person does (20:12) "evil"; of what comes upon him it stays "disaster" (2:11). hikhchid "conceal", the same English as kichad (6:10). chamal al (20:13) "spare", as listed (6:10). azav "abandon" both times (20:13, 19): he will not abandon evil, and he abandoned the poor. mana (20:13; 22:7; 31:16; 38:15) "keep back" (atsar be- stays "hold back"). betokh (20:13) "within". chekh "palate", as listed.
- lechem "bread", as listed. me'ayim (20:14; 30:27) "stomach", kept distinct from beten "belly" (20:15, 20, 23) and from qerev (beqirbo "within him"). nehpakh of food (20:14) "turn". merorah "gall", as planned (16:13). peten "cobra"; ef'eh "viper". rosh (20:16) "venom", leaving "poison" for chemah (6:4).
- chayil "wealth" and bala "swallow", as listed. qi "vomit up". horish with a thing as its object (20:15) "turn out": the verb of dispossessing; it is "make inherit" at 13:26. yanaq (20:16) "suck" (3:12 "nurse").
- ra'ah be- "look on", as listed (3:9). pelaggot "streams"; nahar "river" and nachal "torrent", as listed: the three stand in a row and are put as a list. devash "honey"; chem'ah (20:17; 29:6) "curds".
- yaga (20:18; only here) "what he toiled for", beside yaga "toil" (9:29). temurah "exchange", as planned (15:31): chel temurato "the wealth he got in exchange". alas (20:18; 39:13) "have enjoyment", kept distinct from samach "rejoice" and sus "be glad".
- ritstsats (20:19) "crush": it shares its English with dikka (4:19), and katat stays "shatter". The two perfects with no "and" between them are kept so ("he has crushed, he has abandoned"). gazal (20:19; 24:2, 9, 19) "seize" (achaz be- stays "seize by"). banah of a house that already stands (20:19) "build up".
- shalew as a noun (20:20) "ease", beside "at ease" (16:12). chamud (20:20) "what he desires", beside nechmad "desirable". millet with no object (20:20) "escape", as in Amos 2:15; with an object it stays "deliver" (6:23).
- sarid "survivor", as planned (18:19): en-sarid le'okhlo "There is no survivor from his eating". al-ken "that is why", as listed. chil (20:21) "endure", kept distinct from qum "last" (15:29). tuv (20:21; 21:16) "good", the same English as tov and tovah.
- sefeq (20:22; 36:18) "plenty". bimlot "When ... is full", so that "full" and "fill" are heard in 20:11, 22 and 23. yetser lo "he will be in distress", beside tsar "distress" (7:11); tsarar of steps stays "be cramped" (18:7). amel "one in misery", as listed (3:20): kol-yad amel "the hand of everyone in misery". bo with a suffix "come upon", as planned (15:21).
- yehi le- with an infinitive (20:23) "is to be": yehi lemalle vitno "When his belly is to be filled", left as open as the Hebrew (he is about to fill it, or it is to be filled with what follows). shillach be- (20:23; Ps 78:49) "send on". charon af "the burning of his anger", beside "make his anger burn" (19:11). himtir "rain" (verb), beside matar (5:10), with "it" supplied. alemo (20:23; 22:2; 27:23) "on him". lechum (20:23; Zeph 1:17) "food" [alt: flesh] (decision log, call: ask).
- nesheq (20:24; 39:21) "weapon". qeshet "bow". nechushah "bronze", the same English as nechoshet and nachush. chalaf with a person as its object (20:24) "pierce" (Judg 5:26); elsewhere it stays "sweep on" (9:11).
- shalaf (20:25) "draw out", its object ("it") supplied. gewah (20:25; in this sense only here) "back", like gew (Isa 50:6); "his" is supplied. baraq of a weapon (20:25) "the flashing point": the word is "lightning" (38:35), said of the gleam of a blade (Deut 32:41; Hab 3:11), and "point" is supplied. emim, the plural of emah (9:34) "terrors": it shares its English with ballahot and bi'utim.
- 20:26: tamun "hidden", as listed, and tsefunav "what he has hidden away", tsafan as planned (10:13), so that the two roots are heard side by side. nuppach "blown on": esh lo-nuppach "a fire that no one blew on". yera (20:26) "go ill with".
- gillah "uncover", as planned (12:22), and hitqomem le- (20:27; 27:7) "rise up against", beside qum be- "rise against" (16:8): Zophar answers "Earth, do not cover my blood" and "my witness is in the heavens" (16:18-19). galah (the simple form; 20:28) "go into exile"; one bracketed note at 20:28 says that it is the same verb as "uncover" (20:27).
- yevul "produce". niggarot (20:28) "poured away": it shares "pour" with natakh and shafakh. yom appo "the day of his anger".
- cheleq of what God allots (20:29) "portion", as the Job 17 line planned. nachalah (20:29; 27:13; 31:2; 42:15) "inheritance", beside honchal "be made to inherit" (7:3). nachalat imro "the inheritance decreed for him": emer is a thing said (6:10), here what God has said about him. me'elohim and me'El (20:29) "from God" and "by God".

## Settled in Job 21
- Hebrew text: the Masoretic Text, read by its Ketiv. 21:13: the Ketiv yevallu "wear out" is followed; the Qere yekhallu "bring to an end" (the wording of 36:11) is not noted, since either way all their days are spent in good. 21:20: the Qere enav for the written eno changes only the spelling of "his eyes", since the verb is plural, as at 5:18. The main text keeps to the Masoretic vowels and letters, and one reading that differs from them is an alt, with an entry in the decision log: 21:24 chalav "milk" [alt: the same letters read as chelev "fat", with atinav as a part of the body, as the old Greek and Latin have it] (call: close). Four places where the Hebrew can be read in two ways have alts, each with an entry in the decision log: 21:13 berega "in a moment" [alt: in peace, the sense the old Greek gives the word] (call: clear); 21:16 hen lo veyadam tuvam as a statement, "their good is not in their hand" [alt: a question, "is their good not in their hand?"] (call: close); 21:17 chavalim "pangs" [alt: lots] (call: close); 21:30 leyom ed yechasekh ra, leyom avarot yuvalu "spared in the day of calamity ... led away in the day of fury" [alt: kept for the day of calamity ... led to the day of fury] (call: ask). Not noted: 21:4 we'im taken as "and if it is" (either way his complaint is not to a human being, and he has reason to be impatient); 21:12 ke- before tof, where other Hebrew copies have be- "with" (the sense is the same); 21:13 yechattu taken from chatat "be shattered", which She'ol with no preposition does not go with (7:9) and the old Greek and Latin do not have; 21:17-18 kammah as an exclamation ("How often the lamp of the wicked is snuffed out!"), which the speech does not allow (21:7, 31-33); 21:18 as a wish in the words of Ps 35:5 ("Let them be like straw"), since question and wish both say that it does not happen to them now, as at 14:4; 21:19 ono as "his riches" (20:10), which the wish that follows and 21:21 do not allow; 21:20 the change of kido to pido "his misfortune" (12:5), which no old copy has; 21:22 taken as the friends' objection to Job (the words are the same); 21:29 tenakkeru in the other sense of this form, "treat as strange" (Deut 32:27), since either way the signs stand against the friends; 21:32 yishqod with the dead man as its subject ("and he keeps watch over the mound"), since either way his grave is watched over; 21:33 "after him" and "before him" taken of time (all die as he did) and not of a funeral procession, since the English is as open as the Hebrew; and what the old Greek puts differently (21:13 "they fall asleep in the rest of Hades"; 21:16 "he does not watch over the works of the godless"; 21:19 "may his possessions fail his sons"), as in Job 2 to 20.
- Job's seventh speech is 21:2-34, all in this chapter. Words quoted inside it take single marks: the wicked (21:14-15) and the friends (21:19, 28). 21:19a is read as the friends' own view, set down and then answered: it stands in single marks with nothing added, as at 17:12. Put as a question ("Does God hide away his harm for his sons?") it says the same thing, and nothing is noted. "You" is plural (the friends) in 21:2-3a, 5, 27-29 and 34, and singular in 21:3b ("you may mock"), as at 16:3; English does not show the change, and nothing is added (the Job 7 line). The wicked are plural, with "he" in 21:10, 19-21 and 30a-33, as the Hebrew has it (the Job 18 line). God is named in 21:9, 14, 15, 19, 20 and 22, and is "he" with no name in 21:17 and 21:19b (the Job 16 line).
- The Job 5, 16, 17, 18, 19 and 20 decision logs quoted 21:9, 11, 17, 19, 20, 23 and 26 ahead of time, and the chapter keeps their wording.
- Tenses: the imperfects and perfects that tell how the wicked live are put in the English present, as at 15:20-24, and the wayyiqtol of 21:14 with them ("And they say"), as at 5:15. kammah governs all of 21:17-18 and is repeated in English ("How often ..."), as maddua is at 18:3. The imperfects of 21:19b-20 are wishes ("Let him repay", "Let his eyes see", "let him drink"), and we- with an imperfect after them is "so that he may", as at 10:20.
- shim'u shamoa millati (21:2) "Hear my word carefully", the words of 13:17. tanchumot "comforts", as planned (15:11): Job answers "the comforts of God", and nicham "comfort" closes the speech (21:34). nasa with a person as its object (21:3) "bear with". anokhi adabber "I myself will speak" (13:13). hil'ig (21:3) "mock", the same English as la'ag (11:3); the imperfect is "you may mock".
- he'anokhi (21:4), the pronoun set first outside its clause, "As for me", as with hymeis; teshuvotekhem (21:34), set first in the same way, "As for your replies". siach "complaint" and adam "a human being", as listed. we'im before maddua marks the second question, "And why". qatsar ruach (21:4) "be impatient": the idiom ("the spirit is short") is translated by its meaning, the pair of "be patient" (6:11).
- panah el "turn to" (5:1); heshem with no object (21:5) "be appalled", as planned (16:7); sim yad al-peh "put one's hand on one's mouth" (40:4). im with a perfect (21:6) "When", as at 7:4, with no object added to "remember". nivhal "be dismayed" (4:5). pallatsut "shuddering", beside hitpallets "shudder" (9:6), as planned: we'achaz besari pallatsut "and shuddering seizes my flesh" (the Hebrew says that the flesh seizes it, as at 18:20).
- ataq of people (21:7; Ps 6:7) "grow old": it shares its English with hizqin (14:8); of a rock it stays "be moved" (14:18; 18:4). gavar "grow mighty", beside gevurah "might" and gibbor "a mighty man". chayil (21:7) "wealth", as at 5:5; 15:29; 20:15, 18: Job answers "his wealth will not last". Its other sense, "strength", is not noted, since "grow mighty" already says it.
- zera "offspring" and tse'etsa'im "descendants" (21:8), as at 5:25: Job gives the wicked what Eliphaz promised him. nakhon of offspring (21:8; Ps 102:28) "established"; elsewhere it stays "ready" (12:5; 15:23; 18:12), and the echo is not shown. lifnehem immam "before them, with them", both kept; le'enehem "before their eyes".
- battehem shalom (21:9) "Their houses are at peace", the construction of 5:24 ("your tent is at peace"). mippachad "without dread": min as "without" (11:15) and pachad as listed; Job answers 15:21. shevet "rod", as at 9:34.
- shor of a breeding animal (21:10) "bull" ("ox" at 6:5); parah "cow". ibber (the piel of avar; in this sense only here) "breed". hig'il (only here) "fail": the causative of the verb "loathe" (Lev 26:11), not the verb "reclaim" of 3:5, which is spelled with another letter. pillet of a cow (21:10) "calve" (millet stays "deliver", 6:23); shikkel "miscarry" (Gen 31:38; Exod 23:26).
- shillach "send out", as listed; tson "flock"; awilim "young children", as planned (16:11; 19:18); yeladim "children"; riqqed "dance".
- nasa with no object (21:12; Isa 42:2, 11) "lift one's voice", "their voice" supplied, as in 2:12. tof "drum": a hand drum (the alt at 17:6 has the word); ke- before it is "to". kinnor "lyre" and ugav "pipe", as in Gen 4:21; samach "rejoice"; qol of an instrument "sound".
- billah yamim (21:13) "wear out one's days": balah stays "wear out" (13:28), here of using something to the last (Isa 65:22). battov "in good" and battovah (21:25) "of the good", tov and tovah as at 7:7 and 9:25, so that they are heard with tuv "good" (20:21; 21:16). rega "moment", as listed (7:18; 20:5): Job answers Zophar's "for a moment". yechattu (21:13) "go down": the verb nachat (Jer 21:13; the alt at 17:16); it shares its English with yarad (7:9).
- sur min (21:14; 22:17) "depart from", as at 15:30. da'at "knowledge" (21:14, 22). chafets "wish", as planned (9:3): lo chafatsnu "we do not wish for". mah ... ki "What is ..., that", as at 7:17. avad "serve", beside eved "servant". ho'il "gain", as planned (15:3). paga be- (21:15; Jer 7:16; Ruth 1:16) "appeal to", kept distinct from hitchannen "plead with" and tefillah "prayer".
- tuv "good" and atsat resha'im "the counsel of the wicked", as planned (20:21; 10:3). rachaq min "be far from", beside hirchiq "put far away" (11:14). 21:14a and 21:16b return word for word at 22:17-18.
- kammah (21:17) "How often" (7:19 "How long"; 13:23 "How many"). ner "lamp" and nid'akh "be snuffed out", the words of 18:5-6; ed "calamity", as planned (12:5; 18:12); bo al "come upon". chavalim (21:17) "pangs" [alt: lots] (decision log, call: close), kept distinct from ke'ev "pain"; chevel "rope" (18:10) has the same letters. chilleq "portion out", so that it is heard with cheleq "portion" (20:29).
- teven "straw" and mots "chaff", as planned (13:25). ganav of a storm (21:18; 27:20) "steal away". sufah (21:18; 27:20; 37:9) "whirlwind", leaving "storm" for se'arah (9:17).
- tsafan le- (21:19) "hide away for", as planned (10:13; 15:20). ono (21:19) "his harm": awen with "his", as the Job 18 decision log says; on "vigor", "riches" (18:7, 12; 20:10) is written the same way. shillem "repay", as planned (8:6): yeshallem elav "Let him repay it to him", with the object supplied and "to him" set last, where el puts the weight. weyeda "so that he may know it", the object supplied, as at 9:5.
- kid (21:20; only here) "downfall", kept distinct from pid "misfortune", ed "calamity", hawwah "ruin", shod "destruction" and ra'ah "disaster". chemah "wrath", as planned (19:29); shatah min "drink of".
- chefets (21:21; 22:3) "care": mah-cheftso be- "what does he care about". It is the noun of chafets "wish" (21:14), and the echo is not shown. acharav "after him" (21:21, 33). mispar chodashav "the number of his months", the words of 14:5. chutstsats (21:21; only here) "be cut off": the verb is "divide"; taken as "cut in half" the line says the same thing, that his life is over.
- limmed (21:22) "teach": it shares its English with horah and allef. hal'el, the object set first in a question, "Is it God who ...", as at 13:7-8 and 14:3; the unnamed subject is put in the passive, as at 8:18. wehu with a finite verb (21:22) "when he himself"; in 21:31 and 21:32 the pronoun only names the subject and is not marked, as at 13:28. ramim "those who are high", left as open as the Hebrew (those in the heavens, 4:18; 15:15, or the high among men). shafat "judge", beside shofet.
- zeh ... wezeh (21:23, 25) "One ... another", as at 1:16. be'etsem tummo (21:23) "in his full strength": etsem "bone" is "the very ..." (Gen 7:13), and tom here is completeness (Isa 47:9), not "blamelessness" (4:6). kullo "wholly". shal'anan (only here) "untroubled", a form of sha'anan (3:18; 12:5), and shalew "at ease" (16:12; 20:20), kept distinct, as the Job 3 line planned.
- atin (21:24; only here) "pail" and chalav "milk" [alt: his body ... fat] (decision log, call: close). male "be full of", as at 20:11, which Job is answering. moach (only here) "marrow"; shuqqah (the passive of the verb "give drink") "be moist".
- nefesh marah (21:25) "a bitter soul", beside mar nefesh "bitter in soul" (3:20). akal be- "eat of". 21:26: yachad "together", al-afar "on the dust", shakhav "lie down" and rimmah "maggots", as planned (17:16; 20:11; 7:5); kissah al "cover".
- machshavah "plan", as listed (5:12). mezimmah (21:27; 42:2) "scheme", kept distinct from zimmah "purpose" (17:11). chamas al (the verb; 21:27) "do violence to", beside chamas "violence" (16:17; 19:7); of a vine it stays "tear off" (15:33). A clause without asher (3:3): "the schemes with which".
- ki tomeru (21:28) "For you say" (19:28 "If you say"). ayyeh "where is" (17:15). nadiv alone, of one person (21:28) "the nobleman", beside "nobles" (12:21). ohel "tent" and mishkan "dwelling", as planned (18:21): ohel mishkenot resha'im "the tent where the wicked had their dwellings". Job is quoting the friends (8:22; 18:21; 20:7).
- overe derekh (21:29) "those who pass along the way"; ot "sign", as listed. nikker (the piel; 21:29; 34:19) "recognize", the same English as hikkir (2:12). halo governs both halves of 21:29, as at 7:1, and ki (21:30) is "that", after a colon.
- ra alone, of one person (21:30) "the evil man", like rasha (15:20). nechsakh of a person (21:30) "be spared" [alt: be kept]: it shares its English with chamal (6:10); of pain it stays "be restrained" (16:6). yom ed "the day of calamity". evrah (21:30; 40:11) "fury", leaving "wrath" for chemah, "anger" for af and "resentment" for ka'as; the plural avarot is "fury". huval of the living (21:30) "be led"; of the dead it stays "be carried" (10:19; 21:32). One bracketed note at 21:32 says that the two are the same verb.
- higgid al-panav (21:31) "tell to his face" (al panekha, 1:11). wehu asah, a clause without asher set first and picked up by "him", takes a dash, as at 3:4: "And what he has done—who repays him for it?", with "for it" supplied.
- qevarot "the graves", the plural kept, as planned (17:1). gadish (21:32) "mound": the word is "a stack of grain" (5:26), here the heap raised over a grave, and the bracketed note says so, since Job is taking up Eliphaz's promise ("You will come to the grave ... as a stack of grain goes up in its season"). shaqad al "keep watch over": it shares its English with shamar (10:12); the unnamed subject is put in the passive, as at 8:18.
- mataq (21:33) "be sweet", the same English as himtiq (20:12): Job answers Zophar's "evil is sweet in his mouth". regev (21:33; 38:38) "clod": it shares its English with gush (7:5). nachal as the place of a grave (21:33) "torrent bed", keeping "torrent" (6:15). kol-adam "every human being"; mashakh acharav "follow after": the verb is "draw", used of people moving off in a body (Judg 4:6; 20:37). en mispar "without number" (5:9 "beyond number").
- ekh (21:34) "how can". hevel "in vain", as planned (9:29). teshuvah (21:34; 34:36) "reply", beside heshiv "reply to" (13:22; 20:2), kept distinct from anah "answer". nish'ar "be left". ma'al (21:34; only here in Job) "unfaithfulness": breaking faith with someone (Lev 6:2; Num 5:12), kept distinct from bagad "be treacherous" (6:15).

## Settled in Deuteronomy 5
- Deuteronomy 5 is the first chapter of the book to be translated (Obay, 2026-11-23: chapter 5 first, then chapter 1 and on). Chapters 1-4 follow these lines when they come.
- Hebrew text: the Masoretic Text, read by its Ketiv. 5:10: the Ketiv mitswotaw "his commandments" is followed (the same written word stands at 7:9 and 8:2, where the Qere only adds the letter of the plural; read as a singular it would be "his commandment", and the number is not noted), and the Qere mitswotay "my commandments", the wording of Exod 20:6, is the alt, with an entry in the decision log (call: ask). The two sets of accents in 5:6-21 are not followed for the verse division: the verses are the 33 of the source text, which are those of English Bibles. The paragraphs of 5:6-21 follow the breaks in the Hebrew text, except the one inside 5:21, which the file format cannot show. The main text keeps to the Masoretic letters, vowels and accents: 5:8 has no "and" before kol-temunah (Exod 20:4 has it) and the accent divides pesel from it, so it is "a carved image, any form that is ...", the comma leaving the tie between the two as open as the Hebrew; 5:9 to'ovdem is "serve them" (the same pointing is "let us serve them" at 13:2); 5:5 devar YHWH is singular, "the word of Yahweh". Not noted: 5:22 welo yasaf, which an old Aramaic version takes as "and did not cease" (the vowels make it the verb "add", as at 4:2 and 5:25).
- Three places where the Hebrew can be read in two ways have alts, each with an entry in the decision log: 5:7 al panay "before my face" [alt: besides me] (call: close); 5:11 lashaw "in vain" [alt: for a falsehood] (call: ask); 5:17 ratsach "murder" [alt: kill] (call: clear). Weighed and not bracketed: 5:6 anokhi YHWH elohekha as "I, Yahweh, am your God" (the same thing is said either way); 5:11 nasa shem as "bear the name"; 5:21 chamad as "covet" (a second English wording).
- Quotation marks: Moses' speech opens in 5:1 and runs on to the end of chapter 26, so every paragraph of it opens with a quotation mark, the chapter ends with no closing mark, and chapter 6 opens with one (the Job 4 line). Words quoted inside it take single marks, each paragraph of them opening with both marks (Job 4:17; 10:3): Yahweh's (5:6-21, 28-31) and the people's (5:24-27). Words inside those take double marks again (5:30). Yahweh's words to Moses close at 5:31, because 5:32 speaks to the people of "Yahweh your God" (the John 3 line). 5:5 is Moses' aside inside the sentence and stands in parentheses (the John 2 line), so that "saying" goes with "Yahweh spoke with you" (5:4), as 5:22 says: "These words Yahweh spoke to your whole assembly".
- "You" is singular in the ten words (5:6-21) and in "Hear, Israel" (5:1), and plural in the rest of the chapter; English does not show the change, and nothing is added (the Job 7 line).
- Commands: lo with an imperfect "You must not" (Gen 2:17; 3:1, 3), kept for every prohibition of the ten words, and lo yihyeh lekha (5:7) "You must have no ..."; al with a jussive stays "Do not" (Job 5:22). An infinitive absolute as a command (5:12 shamor) and an imperative (5:16 kabbed) are both plain imperatives. An imperfect that commands is "you will" (5:13), "you are to" (5:31) or "you must" (5:33), by the sentence. A weqatal after a command carries it on: "and learn them and be careful to do them" (5:1); "And remember" (5:15); "And you must be careful" (5:32). The "And" that opens 5:18-21 is kept (Exod 20:14-17 has none).
- YHWH elohenu "Yahweh our God"; YHWH elohekha / elohekhem "Yahweh your God". anokhi YHWH elohekha (5:6) "I am Yahweh your God"; with a predicate after it (5:9) "I, Yahweh your God, am ...". el qanna "a jealous God": El is "God" (Job 5:8) and qanna is the adjective of qin'ah "jealousy" (Job 5:2). elohim acherim "other gods"; elohim chayyim (5:26) "the living God".
- Names: Mosheh "Moses"; Yisra'el "Israel"; Chorev "Horeb"; Mitsrayim "Egypt". shema Yisra'el (5:1; 6:4; 9:1; 20:3; 27:9) "Hear, Israel", with no "O" (the Job 16 line on erets).
- qara el "call to". dibber be'ozne "speak in the ears of": the picture is kept. hayyom "today"; hayyom hazzeh (5:24) "this day". lamad "learn", beside limmed "teach" (Job 21:22). shama "hear" all through the chapter (5:1, 23-28), so that the people's "we will hear it and do it" (5:27) is heard with "Hear, Israel"; "it" is supplied there twice.
- chuqqim "statutes"; mishpatim "judgments" (in Job mishpat stays "justice" or "case"); mitswah "commandment" (the same English as entole), and kol hammitswah (5:31; 6:25; 8:1; 11:8, 22) "the whole commandment", singular as in the Hebrew; tsiwwah "command" (Gen 2:16). davar "word": haddevarim ha'elleh (5:22) "these words". The heading over 5:6-22 is "The ten words", Scripture's own name for them (aseret haddevarim, 4:13; 10:4; Exod 34:28).
- shamar with commandments, a covenant or a day as its object "keep" (5:10, 12, 29): it shares its English with tereo, and "guard" and "watch over" stay for a person or a place. shamar la'asot (5:1, 32; 6:3, 25) "be careful to do": the idiom ("keep to do") is translated by its meaning, and its echo with "keep" is not shown; where the two verbs stand side by side (4:6; 7:12) "keep and do". asah with statutes or commandments "do"; asah with a day (5:15) "observe"; asah chesed le- "show faithful love to" (chesed of God "faithful love", the Job 10 line).
- karat berit im / et "make a covenant with": the idiom ("cut a covenant") is translated by its meaning. lo et-avotenu ... ki ittanu (5:3) "It was not with our fathers that ..., but with us": the words set first are kept first. ittanu anachnu "with us, we ourselves"; elleh foh hayyom "these here today"; kullanu chayyim "all of us alive". avot "fathers".
- panim befanim (5:4) "face to face". al panay (5:7; Exod 20:3) "before my face" [alt: besides me] (decision log, call: close). bahar "at the mountain" of where Yahweh spoke (5:4, 22), and "on the mountain" with alah "go up" (5:5). mittokh "from the midst of" (betokh stays "among" at Job 1:6). esh "fire"; anan "cloud"; arafel "thick darkness", as the Job 3 line planned; choshekh "darkness". ba'ar ba'esh "burn with fire" (ba'ar be- of what a fire burns stays "burn among", Job 1:16); akal of fire "devour", as listed.
- higgid "tell", as listed. yare mippene (5:5) "be afraid of", the same English as gur mippene (the Job 19 line); yare with God as its object (5:29) "fear". ba'et hahi "at that time". amad "stand"; amad ben ... uven "stand between ... and".
- hotsi "bring out": hotsi me'erets Mitsrayim "bring out of the land of Egypt". bet avadim (5:6; 6:12; 7:8; 8:14; 13:5, 10; Exod 13:3, 14; 20:2) "the house of slaves". eved is "slave" every time in this chapter (5:6, 14, 15, 21), as the Job 3 line allows for one who is owned, because the reason for the Sabbath turns on the one word: "so that your male slave and your female slave may rest as you do. And remember that you were a slave in the land of Egypt". eved and amah side by side "male slave" and "female slave" (amah stays "female servant" beside eved "servant", Job 19:15).
- pesel "carved image" (the verb pasal, 10:1, 3, is to be "carve"), kept distinct from tselem "image" and eidolon "idol". temunah "form", as at Job 4:16. shamayim beside the earth and the waters (5:8) "the heavens", as in Gen 1:1; mimma'al "above"; mittachat "below" and mittachat le- "under"; erets beside them "the earth". The "and" that joins the members of a list in a prohibition is "or" (5:8, 14, 21).
- hishtachawah "bow down", as listed. avad with a god as its object "serve" (Job 21:15); avad of work (5:13) "labor" (it shares its English with kopiao, and its echo with eved is not shown). melakhah "work", as in Gen 2:2-3.
- paqad avon avot al banim (5:9; Exod 20:5; 34:7; Num 14:18) "punish the children for the guilt of the fathers": the idiom ("visit the guilt on") is translated by its meaning, as it would be in Hos 1:4 and Amos 3:2; elsewhere paqad stays "inspect". avon here "guilt". banim of both sexes "children" (Gen 3:16). shilleshim and ribbe'im "the third and the fourth generation", with "generation" supplied. la'alafim "to thousands", left as open as the Hebrew (thousands of people, or of generations, as 7:9 puts it: "to a thousand generations"). leson'ay "of those who hate me" and le'ohavay "of those who love me": "of" leaves open, as the Hebrew does, whether the fathers are meant or the generations after them. sane "hate" and ahav "love", as listed.
- nasa shem (5:11; Exod 20:7) "take up the name": nasa of taking a word on one's lips (Ps 16:4; Exod 23:1). lashaw "in vain" [alt: for a falsehood] (decision log, call: ask): as a noun shaw stays "emptiness" (Job 7:3), and "in vain" is shared with hevel as an adverb (Job 9:29; 21:34). ed shaw (5:20) "a false witness", with one bracketed note that the word is the "in vain" of 5:11; ed shaqer (Exod 20:16) is to be "a lying witness", so that the two chapters are told apart. niqqah "hold innocent", as listed.
- shabbat "Sabbath", capitalized, with "a" where the Hebrew has no article (5:14); yom hashabbat "the Sabbath day". qiddesh "make holy" (Gen 2:3; Job 1:5): leqaddesho "to make it holy". sheshet yamim "six days", with no "for". yom hashevi'i "the seventh day".
- shor "ox" and chamor "donkey"; behemah "livestock", as listed; wekhol behemtekha "or any of your livestock". ger "resident foreigner": one who lives among a people that is not his own (the noun of gur, Job 18:19; 19:15), kept distinct from nokhri "foreigner" and zar "stranger"; gerekha "your resident foreigner". bish'arekha "within your gates": the gates stand for the towns, and the picture is kept. lema'an with an imperfect "so that ... may". nuach "rest", as listed (shavat stays "cease"). kamokha (5:14) "as you do".
- zakar "remember", as listed. beyad chazaqah uvizroa netuyah (5:15; 4:34; 26:8; with the article or "his" at 7:19; 11:2) "with a strong hand and with an outstretched arm". misham "from there". al-ken "that is why", as listed.
- kibbed "honor" (kaved "be honored", Job 14:21). he'erikh yamim: with the days as its subject (5:16; 6:2; 25:15) "your days may be long"; with a person as its subject (5:33; 4:26, 40; 11:9; 17:20; 22:7; 30:18; 32:47) "lengthen one's days". yatav le- (5:16, 29) "go well with"; tov le- (5:33) "be good for". adamah "ground", as in Genesis, kept distinct from erets "land": al ha'adamah asher (5:16) "on the ground which", with "which" and not "that", so that it is not heard as "on the ground that", meaning "because". noten (the participle) "is giving".
- ratsach (5:17) "murder" [alt: kill] (decision log, call: clear), kept distinct from harag "kill", hemit "put to death" and hikkah "strike down"; of one who kills without meaning to (4:42; 19:3-6) another wording will be needed. na'af "commit adultery"; ganav "steal" (of a storm "steal away", Job 21:18). anah be- "testify against" (Job 15:6); ed "witness", as listed: the witness is the person, and "as" is supplied. rea in the Law "neighbor": the one a person lives beside and deals with (in Job, of the three, it stays "friend"). eshet re'ekha "your neighbor's wife".
- chamad (5:21; Exod 20:17) "desire", not "covet": it is the verb of nechmad "desirable" (Gen 2:9; 3:6) and chamud "what he desires" (Job 20:20), and it shares its English with epithymia "desire". hit'awweh (5:21) "crave", kept distinct from it (the noun ta'avah stays "delight", Gen 3:6, and the echo is not shown). sadeh "field". kol asher lere'ekha "anything that is your neighbor's".
- qahal "assembly": kol qehalkhem "your whole assembly". qol gadol (5:22) "with a great voice": qol is "voice" all through the chapter where it is Yahweh's, and "sound" in qol divrekhem "the sound of your words" (5:28; qol devarim, 4:12). welo yasaf "and he added no more"; yasaf with an infinitive and od (5:25) "go on ... any longer". luchot avanim "tablets of stone"; katav "write".
- wayehi with ke- and an infinitive (5:23) "And when" (the Genesis 4 line). A clause with we- and a participle beside it (wehahar bo'er) "while". qarav "come near" (5:23) and "go near" (5:27), by who is speaking. rosh of the leaders of the tribes (5:23; 1:15) "head": rashe shivtekhem "the heads of your tribes", so that "head" can stay one word through the book (1:13, 15; 28:13, 44); "chief" stays at Job 12:24. shevet "tribe" ("rod" at Job 9:34; 21:9). zaqen "elder", as listed.
- her'ah "show"; kavod "glory", as listed; godel "greatness". ha'adam of anyone (5:24) "a human being", as adam is in Job; wachay "and he lives". kol basar (5:26) "all flesh". An object set first is kept first where English allows it (5:22 "These words Yahweh spoke"; 5:24 "and his voice we have heard").
- we'attah lammah (5:25) "And now, why". im with a participle (5:25) "If". mi khol-basar asher (5:26) "who is there of all flesh that". kamonu "as we have".
- attah and att as emphatic subjects (5:27) "You yourself ... and you yourself", as listed; we'attah set first against others (5:31) "But as for you". amar "say" and dibber "speak" are kept distinct all through (5:27 "all that Yahweh our God says ... all that Yahweh our God speaks to you"); dibber with an object and el (5:27, 31) "speak ... to".
- hetiv with what was said as its object (5:28; 18:17) "do well in" ("do good", Gen 4:7). mi yitten wehayah levavam zeh lahem (5:29) "If only this heart of theirs were theirs": mi yitten "If only", as listed; levavam zeh "this heart of theirs", the heart they have just shown. kol hayyamim "always" and le'olam "forever", as listed.
- lekh emor (5:30) "Go, say", two commands as in the Hebrew. shuv "return"; ohel "tent"; lakhem after the command is not translated. poh "here"; immadi of place (5:31) "with me". A cohortative after an imperative (5:31) "and I will". asher telammedem we'asu "that you are to teach them, and they are to do them".
- yarash "take possession of" (lerishtah, 5:31; tirashun, 5:33), kept distinct from nachal "inherit"; horish, of driving a people out, will need a wording of its own (it is "turn out" at Job 20:15 and "make inherit" at Job 13:26). erets of the land promised "land".
- sur with yamin usmol (5:32; 17:11, 20; 28:14) "turn aside to the right or to the left" (sur min stays "turn away from" or "depart from"). halakh bederekh "walk in the way": bekhol hadderekh asher tsiwwah (5:33) "in all the way that ... has commanded". ka'asher tsiwwekha / tsiwwah YHWH "as Yahweh your God has commanded you": the perfect is put in the English perfect (5:12, 15, 16, 32, 33). lema'an tichyun "so that you may live".
- For Exodus 20, which is not translated yet: where its Hebrew is the same as this chapter's, the English is to be the same word for word. That is Exod 20:2-3 (= 5:6-7); 20:5-6 (= 5:9-10, but with no "and" before "the third", and with "my commandments" written); 20:7 (= 5:11); 20:9 (= 5:13); and the words the two share in 20:4, 10 and 12-17. The differences are to be heard: 20:4 "or any form"; 20:8 zakhor "Remember" for shamor "Keep"; 20:10 has "or your livestock" for "or your ox or your donkey or any of your livestock", and no "so that your male slave and your female slave may rest as you do"; 20:11 gives the six days of creation as the reason, where 5:15 gives Egypt; 20:8 and 20:12 have no "as Yahweh your God has commanded you", and 20:12 has no "and so that it may go well with you"; 20:13-17 have no "And"; 20:16 "a lying witness"; 20:17 has "desire" twice, with the house before the wife and no field. Whether the brackets of 5:7, 5:11 and 5:17 are written again there is for that chapter's translator.

## Settled in Genesis 6
- Hebrew text: the Masoretic Text; the chapter has no Qere. The main text keeps to the Masoretic vowels: 6:3 beshaggam is be- with she- and gam, "because ... too"; the pointing that other Hebrew copies have, which makes it "in their going astray", is the alt (decision log, call: close). Not noted: 6:13 et-ha'arets taken as a second object ("ruin them and the earth", as the old Greek has it), since the sense is the same; 6:14 qinnim read with other vowels as "reeds", which no old copy has; 6:16, where the suffix of tekhalennah is feminine, as the ark is (the gender of tsohar, which stands only here, is not known), and English "it" leaves open what is finished.
- ha'adam all through 6:1-7 "humankind", the race, as in 1:26-27 and Job 7:20. The Hebrew says "them" of it in 6:1 and "he" in 6:3 and 6:5; English says "they", "their" for both. adam with no article in me'adam ad-behemah (6:7; 7:23) "from humankind to beasts".
- wayehi ki (6:1) "And when", as listed; the sentence runs on into 6:2. hechel "begin". larov (6:1; the infinitive of ravav) "multiply", the same English as ravah (1:22, 28): two forms of one root. Its echo with rabbah "great" (6:5) is not shown. al-pene ha'adamah "on the face of the ground"; me'al pene ha'adamah (6:7) "off the face of the ground".
- bene ha'elohim "the sons of God", as at Job 1:6, with no alt and no note; benot ha'adam "the daughters of humankind".
- tov of a person's looks (6:2) "beautiful", with one bracketed note that it is the word "good" and that "saw", "good" and "took" are the words of 3:6. laqach lo nashim "take wives for themselves", as at 4:19. mikkol asher bacharu "whichever they chose": min with kol asher is put by its meaning. bachar "choose".
- yadon (6:3; the form stands only here) "remain in" [alt: contend with] (decision log, call: ask). ruach of God in humankind (6:3) "spirit", lowercase. ruach chayyim (6:17; 7:15) "the spirit of life", kept distinct from nishmat chayyim "the breath of life" (2:7), so that nishmat ruach chayyim (7:22) can be "the breath of the spirit of life". le'olam "forever", as at 3:22. basar "flesh"; kol basar "all flesh". wehayu yamav (6:3) "and their days will be": the English leaves open, as the Hebrew does, whether this is the length of a life or the time left before the flood.
- hannefilim (6:4; Num 13:33) "the Nephilim": a name, as English has it, like the cherubim and the peoples of Deut 2:10-11 [alt at 6:4: the giants, or the fallen ones] (decision log, call: clear). Under the rule against standing brackets the bracket is not repeated at Num 13:33.
- wegam acharei-khen asher (6:4) "and also afterward, when": the English leaves open, as the Hebrew does, whether "when" goes with "those days" or with "afterward". The imperfect and the weqatal after it are "would", as at Job 1:4-5. hemmah "These", left as open as the Hebrew (the Nephilim, or the children).
- bo el of a man and a woman (6:4) "sleep with": the idiom ("come in to") is translated by its meaning, as Obay decided for yada on 2026-11-21. This takes the place of the "go in to" that the Genesis 4 line planned; "lie with" stays for shakhav im. It is the translator's call, and Obay can switch it. yalad le- of the mother "bear children to", as at 3:16.
- gibbor "mighty man", as planned (Job 16:14). asher me'olam "who were of old". anshe hashshem "the men of renown": the idiom ("the men of the name") is translated by its meaning (Num 16:2).
- ra'ah of what people do (6:5) "evil" and ra "evil", as at Job 1:1 and 20:12 (tov wara stays "good and bad"). yetser (6:5; 8:21) "what is formed": kol yetser machshevot libbo "everything formed in the thoughts of their heart", so that it is heard with yatsar "form" (2:7, 8, 19); the word is a thing formed in Isa 29:16 and Ps 103:14, and a design that a person is making in Deut 31:21. machshavah (6:5) "thought"; "plan" stays in Job (5:12). lev "heart", singular as in the Hebrew. raq "only". kol hayyom "all day long" (kol hayyamim stays "always").
- nicham (the niphal; 6:6, 7) "regret", not softened. One bracketed note at 6:6 says that it is the verb of "bring comfort" (5:29) and that "pained" is from the root of "painful toil". hit'atstsev "be pained", beside etsev "pain" and itsavon "painful toil"; el-libbo "to his heart".
- machah "wipe": machah me'al pene ha'adamah (6:7; 7:4) "wipe off the face of the ground". It shares "wipe" with nikhchad "be wiped out" (Job 4:7).
- behemah of the animals as a whole, beside creeping things and birds (6:7, 20) "beasts", as the Job 12 line says; "livestock" stays where they are set beside wild animals (7:14, 21). One bracketed note at 6:7 says that it is the word for "livestock" in chapter 1. 6:20 takes up 1:25, and the rest of its wording is the same ("according to their kinds", "every creeping thing of the ground according to its kind").
- matsa chen be'ene (6:8) "find favor in the eyes of"; chen "favor" (leaving "grace" for charis), with one bracketed note that it is the name "Noah" written backward.
- elleh toledot Noach (6:9) "These are the generations of Noah", as at 2:4 and 5:1, with no bracket (no standing brackets). dor "generation" (Job 8:8): bedorotav "in his generations", so that 7:1 ("in this generation") can be heard with it. It shares its English with toledot, and one bracketed note says that it is a different word. tsaddiq "righteous" and tamim "blameless", as listed (Job 12:4). et-ha'elohim hithallekh-Noach "With God Noah walked": the words set first are kept first, and "walked with God" is the English of 5:22, 24.
- shachat "ruin" in every form, so that the five in the chapter are heard together: the niphal (6:11, 12) "be ruined", as of a garment or a pot (Jer 13:7; 18:4); the hiphil with derekh (6:12) "ruin one's way"; the hiphil and the piel of what God does (6:13, 17; 9:11, 15) "ruin". It shares its English with chubbal (Job 17:1). lifne ha'elohim "before God". chamas "violence", as listed (Job 16:17). nimla (6:11) "be filled with"; male (6:13) "be full of".
- wayyar elohim ... wehinneh (6:12) "And God saw ..., and look, it was", the English of 1:31. darko (6:12) "its way": the way of all flesh.
- qets "end", as listed; ba lefanay "has come before me". mippenehem "because of them". hineni with a participle (6:13, 17) "look, I am about to". mashchitam et-ha'arets "ruin them along with the earth": et as "with", as in 6:9 and 6:18.
- tevah "ark": the English word for this vessel (the word returns at Exod 2:3, 5; aron is another word). atse-gofer (only here) "gopher wood": the name of the wood, as English has it, since Scripture does not say what tree it is. qinnim "compartments" (the word is "nests"). kafar (the simple form; only here) "cover" and kofer "pitch"; one bracketed note at 6:14 says that the two share a root and that the name of the wood sounds like them. mibbayit umichuts "inside and outside".
- Commands in 6:14-21: an imperative is a plain imperative; an imperfect that commands is "you are to" (Deut 5:31), and a weqatal after it carries it on.
- ammah "cubit": the unit is translated, not converted and not explained, like metretes and stadion. orekh "length"; rochav "width"; qomah "height" (it shares its English with govah). In 6:15 the measures are set after their nouns in English.
- tsohar (6:16; only here) "an opening for light" [alt: a roof] (decision log, call: close); challon (8:6) stays "window". killah "finish", as at 2:2; el-ammah ... milma'lah "to a cubit at the top", left as open as the Hebrew. petach "entrance", as listed; tsad "side"; sim "put". tachtiyyim sheniyyim ushelishim "lower, second, and third decks", with "decks" supplied.
- wa'ani set first (6:17) "And as for me" and we'attah (6:21) "And as for you", as at Deut 5:31. mabbul "flood": hammabbul mayim "the flood of water", the apposition put with "of". mittachat hashamayim "from under the sky", as at 1:9. gava "breathe its last", as listed.
- heqim berit (6:18; 9:9, 11, 17; 17:7) "establish a covenant", kept distinct from karat berit "make a covenant" (Deut 5:2). bo el of a place "go into"; hevi el "bring into".
- kol hachay "every living thing". shenayim mikkol "two of each", with no "kind" added (min stays "kind"). hecheyah "keep alive"; lehachayot with no object (6:20) "to be kept alive". zakhar uneqevah "male and female", as at 1:27.
- ma'akhal and okhlah both "food", as in chapters 1 to 3; lekha welahem le'okhlah (6:21) "yours and theirs for food", the English of 1:29. asaf "gather" (it shares its English with synago). min with food "some of", as at 3:6.
- 6:22 follows the Masoretic accents: "And Noah did it; according to all that God had commanded him, so he did", with "it" supplied. asah of carrying out a command "do".

## Settled in Genesis 7
- Hebrew text: the Masoretic Text; the chapter has no Qere. The main text keeps to the Masoretic vowels: 7:23 wayyimach is the active form, "he wiped away", with no subject named (the last one named is Yahweh, 7:16), and the English leaves it "he". Not noted, as in Job 2: what the old Greek adds or puts differently (7:2 "two, two" for the beasts that are not clean, and "male and female" both times; 7:3 "the clean birds of the sky", with a further clause, "and of the birds that are not clean, two by two, male and female"; 7:11 the twenty-seventh day for the seventeenth; 7:17 "and forty nights"). In 7:3 the Masoretic text names the birds without dividing them; its "Also" can be heard as carrying the rule of 7:2 over to them (8:20 speaks of clean birds), and the English is as open as the Hebrew.
- Chapter 7 carries out 6:13-22, and the same Hebrew is the same English: tevah "ark", mabbul "flood", kol basar "all flesh", ruach chayyim "the spirit of life", gava "breathe its last", machah "wipe", zakhar uneqevah "male and female", "according to their kinds". 7:5 wayya'as Noach kekhol asher-tsiwwahu YHWH "And Noah did according to all that Yahweh had commanded him" (6:22); ka'asher tsiwwah (7:9, 16) "as God had commanded".
- bo el-hattevah of Noah and his household (7:1, 7, 13) "go into the ark", as at 6:18; bo el-Noach el-hattevah of the animals (7:9, 15) "come to Noah into the ark", as 6:20 said they would ("will come to you"). habba'im ... ba'u (7:16) "those that came, came", the repetition kept.
- bayit of the people of a house (7:1) "household" (the same English as oikia): kol-betkha "all your household". An object set first (7:1 ki-otkha ra'iti) "for it is you I have seen", as at Deut 5:3. ra'ah with a second object "see to be". lefanay "before me" (6:11, 13). baddor hazzeh "in this generation", as the Genesis 6 line planned.
- behemah in chapter 7, as the Genesis 6 line planned: "beasts" where it is the animals as a whole, clean and not clean, beside birds and creeping things (7:2, 8), and in me'adam ad-behemah (7:23, the words of 6:7); "livestock" where it stands beside the wild animals (7:14, 21). The note of 6:7 is not repeated (no standing brackets). tahor of animals "clean", as at Job 14:4; asher lo tehorah hi (7:2) and asher enennah tehorah (7:8) both "that are not clean" (English has no second plain wording).
- A number said twice is "so many each". shiv'ah shiv'ah (7:2, 3) "seven of each" [alt at 7:2: seven pairs] (decision log, call: ask); the bracket stands once, and 7:3 follows the same call. shenayim shenayim with "came" (7:9, 15) "two by two"; shenayim mikkol (6:19, 20) stays "two of each", so that the two phrases are told apart.
- ish we'ishto of animals (7:2) "a male and its mate": the idiom ("a man and his wife") is translated by its meaning, with no note, and kept distinct from zakhar uneqevah "male and female" (7:3, 9, 16).
- chiyyah zera (7:3; 19:32, 34) "keep offspring alive": the piel shares its English with hecheyah (6:19, 20); zera of the animals' line "offspring", as listed. al-pene khol-ha'arets "on the face of the whole earth", the English of 1:29.
- leyamim od shiv'ah (7:4) "in seven more days"; leshiv'at hayyamim (7:10) "after the seven days", those of 7:4. anokhi with a participle of what is about to happen (7:4) "I am going to" (hineni with a participle stays "look, I am about to", 6:13, 17). himtir "make it rain", as at 2:5. arba'im yom we'arba'im laylah "forty days and forty nights". geshem (7:12) "rain": it shares its English with matar.
- yequm (7:4, 23; Deut 11:6) "being": kol-hayqum "every being". The word is what stands or exists, and in 7:23 and Deut 11:6 it is living things; "being" is kept distinct from kol hachay "every living thing" (6:19) and nefesh chayyah "living creature".
- machah, continued: with me'al (7:4) "wipe off", as at 6:7; with no "off" (7:23) "wipe away" ("wipe out" stays for nikhchad, Job 4:7); the niphal with min (7:23) "be wiped from". The active and the passive of 7:23 keep the one verb.
- weNoach at the head of a sentence that breaks the chain (7:6) "Now Noah". hayah of the flood and its waters arriving (7:6, 10) "come"; wayehi with a length of time (7:12, 17) "was ... for". hammabbul hayah mayim (7:6) "the flood of water came", the English of 6:17. me hammabbul "the waters of the flood". mippene (7:7) "because of", as at 6:13.
- A singular verb with several subjects (7:7, 13): the verb goes with the first, and the others follow it ("And Noah went into the ark, and his sons ... with him"). 7:8-9 and 7:13-14 are each one sentence, kept whole across the verse break.
- Dates: bishnat shesh-me'ot shanah lechayye-Noach "In the six hundredth year of Noah's life"; bachodesh hasheni "in the second month"; beshiv'ah-asar yom lachodesh "on the seventeenth day of the month". bayyom hazzeh in narrative (7:11) "on that day"; be'etsem hayyom hazzeh (7:13) "on that very day", as the Job 21 line said.
- nivqa of springs (7:11; of the deeps, Prov 3:20) "burst open"; baqa elsewhere "split" (Exod 14:16, 21). ma'yan "spring": it shares its English with ed (2:6) and pege. tehom rabbah "the great deep", tehom as at 1:2. arubbot hashamayim (7:11; 8:2) "the windows of the sky": the word is an opening that can be looked through (Eccl 12:3) or let smoke out (Hos 13:3); it shares "window" with challon (8:6), since English has no second plain word. niftach "be opened".
- hachayyah beside behemah (7:14, 21; 8:1) "the wild animals", as chayyat ha'arets is in 1:24-25. In 7:14 leminah is "according to their kinds" and leminehu "according to its kind" of the creeping thing and "according to their kinds" of the birds, the wording of 1:25 and 6:20. kol-haremes haromes al-ha'arets "every creeping thing that creeps on the earth", the English of 1:26.
- tsippor (7:14) "bird": it shares its English with of. kol tsippor kol-kanaf (7:14; Ezek 17:23; compare Ezek 39:4, 17) "every bird of every wing": kanaf "wing" ("winged" at 1:21). Taken as two phrases side by side ("every bird, every winged thing") it says the same thing, since of already covers all that flies.
- A clause with no verb inside the narrative is put in the past (7:15 "in which there was the spirit of life"; 7:22 "that had ... in its nostrils"; 7:19 "that were under the whole sky"). nishmat-ruach chayyim be'appav (7:22) "the breath of the spirit of life in its nostrils", as the Genesis 6 line planned, with appayim "nostrils" as at 2:7.
- sagar be'ad with a person (7:16; 2 Kgs 4:21) "shut in": no "door" is supplied; it shares its English with sagar al (Job 12:14).
- ravah of water (7:17, 18) "increase": "multiply" (1:22, 28) does not fit water, and one bracketed note at 7:17 says that it is the same verb. gavar of the waters (7:18, 19, 20, 24) "prevail", the same English all four times, so that the refrain is heard: the verb is "be strong" and, with al, "prevail over" (49:26), and it lasts a hundred and fifty days (7:24), which "grow mighty" (Job 21:7) would not say. Its echo with gibbor "mighty man" (6:4) is not shown. al-ha'arets stays "on the earth" all through the chapter.
- me'od with a verb (7:18) "greatly"; me'od me'od (7:19) "very greatly". nasa "lift". rum me'al (7:17; Ezek 10:4) "rise up from". halakh al-pene hammayim (7:18) "go over the face of the waters", the English of 1:2.
- kussah (the passive of kissah; 7:19, 20) "be covered": it shares its English with kafar (6:14). har "mountain"; gavoah "high", beside govah "height". tachat kol-hashamayim "under the whole sky" (6:17 "from under the sky").
- milma'lah (7:20) "above" (6:16 "at the top"): chamesh esreh ammah milma'lah "Fifteen cubits above", left as open as the Hebrew, which does not say what the fifteen cubits are measured from; the next words, "and the mountains were covered", are the reason to hear it of the mountains.
- ramas of all flesh (7:21) "move", as at 1:21 and 1:28; kol asher romes beside beasts and birds (7:8) "everything that creeps", as at 1:30. be- before the members of a list (7:21; 8:17) is shown by setting the list between dashes; wekhol ha'adam, which has no be-, stands after it, "and all humankind". sherets hashorets "swarming thing that swarms", beside "swarm" and "swarms" (1:20, 21).
- charavah (7:22; Exod 14:21; Josh 3:17) "the dry ground", kept distinct from yabbashah "dry land" (1:9, 10); the verb charav is "be drained" at Job 14:11, and 8:13 will need it beside yavesh (8:14). mut "die", beside gava "breathe its last" (7:21).
- nish'ar "be left", as at Job 21:34; akh "only" (Job 13:20). wa'asher itto (7:23) "and those with him", people and animals alike. chamishim ume'at yom "a hundred and fifty days", in normal English order (the Genesis 5 line).

## Settled in Genesis 8
- Hebrew text: the Masoretic Text, read by its Ketiv. 8:17: the Ketiv hotse and the Qere haytse are two forms of the same command, "bring out", so nothing is noted. The main text keeps to the Masoretic vowels: 8:10 wayyachel and 8:12 wayyiyyachel are two forms, and both are "waited". Not noted, as in Job 2 and Genesis 7: what the ancient versions add or put differently (8:7, where the old Greek and Latin have "and did not return" of the raven; 8:4, where the old Greek has the twenty-seventh day, as at 7:11; 8:13, where it adds "of Noah's life"; 8:21, where it has "having considered" for "to his heart").
- One place where the Hebrew can be read in two ways has an alt, with an entry in the decision log: 8:21 ki "for" [alt: though] (call: ask). Weighed and not bracketed: 8:1 ruach as "spirit" (a bracketed note gives the echo); 8:11 taraf as "fresh" or as "torn off" (the leaf is the same); 8:21 lo osif leqallel od as "I will no longer curse", as if a curse still in force were being lifted (the verb names an act, and the sentence beside it is about doing again what was done); 8:21 ba'avur as "for the sake of" (3:17 has "because of").
- Chapter 8 undoes chapter 7 step by step, and the same Hebrew is the same English: ma'yenot tehom "the springs of the deep" (7:11 has "all" and "great", 8:2 has neither), arubbot hashamayim "the windows of the sky", geshem "rain", "a hundred and fifty days", "the mountains", tahor "clean", "every creeping thing that creeps on the earth", "all flesh", and the lists of Noah's family, each in the order the Hebrew has it (wife before sons in 8:16; sons before wife in 8:18, as at 6:18 and 7:7).
- zakar "remember", as listed (it returns at 9:15-16). kol-hachayyah beside kol-habbehemah (8:1) "all the wild animals and all the livestock", as the Genesis 7 line planned. kol-hachayyah at the head of a list of birds, beasts and creeping things (8:17), and in the list that carries it out (8:19), "every living thing", as chayyah is at 1:28; behemah under that head (8:17) and of the clean animals (8:20) "beasts", as at 7:2, 8.
- ruach (8:1) "wind", with one bracketed note that it is the word for "spirit" in 1:2. It is a note and not an alt: God makes it pass over the earth and the waters subside, as a wind drives back the sea at Exod 14:21. he'evir with a wind "make pass" (he'evir avon stays "take away guilt", Job 7:21); avar al "pass over".
- shakakh of water (8:1) "subside" (of anger, Esth 2:1; 7:10). niskar (8:2; Ps 63:11) "be stopped up", one verb for the springs and the windows, as in the Hebrew; nikhla (8:2) "be withheld", and kala "withhold" (23:6), kept distinct from atsar be- "hold back" and mana "keep back". haggeshem min-hashamayim "the rain from the sky".
- shuv "return" all through the chapter (8:3, 7, 9, 12), of the waters, the raven and the dove, so that the thread is heard. halokh with the infinitive absolute of the verb (8:3 halokh washov; 8:5 hayu halokh wechasor) "steadily": "returned steadily", "decreased steadily". yatso washov after its own verb (8:7) "going out and returning". me'al ha'arets "from the earth", as at 7:17; me'al pene ha'adamah (8:8) "from the face of the ground".
- chaser of water (8:3, 5) "decrease", the pair of ravah "increase" (7:17-18); elsewhere "lack" (18:28). miqtseh (8:3) and miqqets (8:6) "at the end of", beside qets "end" (6:13); 4:3 has "after".
- nuach of the ark (8:4) "come to rest", beside nuach "rest" (Job 3:13; Deut 5:14); manoach (8:9) "resting place"; nichoach (8:21) "soothing": reach hannichoach "the soothing aroma" (reach nichoach, Exod 29:18 and often in the Law, "a soothing aroma"). One bracketed note at 8:4 says that all three sound like the name "Noah". reach of an offering "aroma" ("scent" at Job 14:9); heriach "smell", and the cognate object is not shown.
- Dates, as in chapter 7: bachodesh hashevi'i beshiv'ah-asar yom lachodesh "in the seventh month, on the seventeenth day of the month"; ba'asiri be'echad lachodesh "In the tenth month, on the first day of the month", with "month" and "day" supplied; be'achat weshesh-me'ot shanah "in the six hundred and first year". hare Ararat "the mountains of Ararat": the name as English has it (2 Kgs 19:37; Jer 51:27).
- nir'ah "appear", as at 1:9; rosh of a mountain "top".
- challon "window", as planned (6:16; 7:11); mikhseh "covering"; hesir "remove", as listed. The Genesis 6 decision log quoted 8:6 and 8:13 ahead of time, and the chapter keeps its wording. asher asah (8:6) "that he had made", as open as the Hebrew (the window, or the ark).
- shillach "send out", as listed; me'itto "from him". orev "raven"; yonah "dove". ha'orev and hayyonah keep their article at the first mention, "the raven", "the dove". Both birds are "it" (the Hebrew has "he" of the raven and "she" of the dove), as animals are at 7:2.
- yavesh of water (8:7) "dry up", as listed; of the earth (8:14) "be dry", so that it is heard with yabbashah "dry land" (1:9-10). charav (8:13, twice) "be drained", as at Job 14:11 and as the Genesis 7 line planned: on the first day of the year the water has run off the ground, and by the twenty-seventh day of the second month the earth is dry. Its echo with charavah "the dry ground" (7:22) is not shown.
- qalal of water (8:8, 11) "go down": the verb is "be slight" ("be swift" at Job 7:6; of a person slighted, 16:4-5); it shares its English with yarad.
- matsa "find", kaf regel "the sole of the foot", shalach yad "reach out one's hand", laqach "take" and hevi el "bring into", as listed. welo at the head of 8:9 "But". mayim with no article (8:9) "water". shuv elav el-hattevah "return to him, to the ark".
- wayyachel / wayyiyyachel "waited" (yichel "wait", Job 6:11). od shiv'at yamim acherim (8:10, 12) "another seven days". hosif with an infinitive (8:10) "again"; lo yasaf with an infinitive and od (8:12) "not ... again"; lo osif ... od in a promise (8:21, twice) "never again" (od stands after the infinitive the first time and before it the second; the English is the same).
- le'et erev "at evening time". aleh "leaf", as listed; zayit "olive". taraf (8:11; as an adjective only here) "freshly plucked": the word of taraf "tear" (Job 16:9), used of new leaves at Ezek 17:9.
- wayyar wehinneh (8:13) "and he saw, and look", as at 6:12.
- dibber el ... lemor "speak to ..., saying". yatsa min "go out of" (8:16, 18, 19), the pair of bo el "go into" (7:1); hotsi "bring out", as listed.
- 8:17: the object set first stays first (Deut 5:22); be- before the members of the list is shown by dashes, as the Genesis 7 line planned; a weqatal of the animals after the command "and let them". sharats "swarm", parah "be fruitful" and ravah "multiply", the English of 1:20-22 and 1:28; ba'arets and al-ha'arets both "on the earth", as in chapters 6 and 7.
- kol romes al-ha'arets as the sum of the list (8:19) "everything that moves on the earth", as at 7:21 (the same words are "everything that creeps on the earth" at 1:30, where they are one member of a list). lemishpechotehem "according to their families": mishpachah "family", kept distinct from min "kind".
- mizbeach "altar"; banah "build", as listed. laqach min "take some of", as at 3:6. he'elah olot "offer up burnt offerings", as at Job 1:5; bammizbeach "on the altar".
- amar el-libbo (8:21) "say to his heart", the el-libbo of 6:6, so that the two places that speak of Yahweh's heart are heard together (amar belibbo, 17:17, will be "say in his heart").
- qillel with Yahweh as its subject (8:21) "bring a curse on": it is not arar "curse", "cursed" (3:14, 17; 4:11; 5:29), and it is given another form so that the two can be told apart, as qavav is at Job 3:8. Of what a person says it stays "curse" (Job 3:1). One bracketed note says that the verb is not the one of 3:17 and that it is from the root of "gone down" (8:8, 11). At 12:3 the two verbs stand in one sentence and will need two wordings; qelalah (27:12-13) "a curse".
- ba'avur "because of", as at 3:17. ha'adam (8:21, twice) "humankind", as in 6:1-7, with "their" for the Hebrew "his". yetser lev ha'adam "what is formed in the heart of humankind", as planned (6:5); 8:21 has no "everything", "thoughts", "only" or "all day long", and the English has none. ra "evil"; ne'urim "youth", as listed. ki after the promise (8:21) "for" [alt: though] (decision log, call: ask).
- hikkah "strike down", as at 4:15; kol-chay "every living thing" (it shares its English with kol-hachayyah, 8:17, 19, and kol hachay, 6:19). ka'asher asiti "as I have done".
- od kol-yeme ha'arets (8:22) "While all the days of the earth last": "last" is supplied, and "all the days of the earth" is kept, so that it is heard with "all the days of your life" (3:17). zera of the time of sowing "seedtime"; qatsir "harvest", as listed; qor "cold"; chom "heat"; qayits "summer"; choref "winter". Every "and" of the four pairs is kept. shavat "cease", as at 2:2-3.
- Yahweh's words run from 8:21 to the end of 8:22: nothing signals a change of speaker before 9:1 (the John 3 line).
- For Genesis 9: 9:1 and 9:7 take up 1:28 and 8:17 ("Be fruitful and multiply", "swarm on the earth"); lo ... od (9:11, 15) is to be "never again", as at 8:21; shachat "ruin", mabbul "flood" and heqim berit "establish a covenant", as the Genesis 6 line planned; zakar (9:15, 16) "remember", as at 8:1; 9:10 has be- before the members of a list again, with behemah beside chayyat ha'arets ("the livestock", "wild animals of the earth"); arur (9:25) is the verb of 3:14 and 3:17, "cursed", not the one of 8:21.

## Settled in Genesis 9
- Hebrew text: the Masoretic Text; the chapter has no Qere. The main text keeps to the Masoretic vowels and accents: 9:2 is divided where the accents divide it, after "every bird of the sky", so that the two phrases with be- go with "into your hand they are given" (be- as "with", as in Exod 10:9); 9:21 oholoh is "his tent", written with the older spelling of "his" (12:8; 13:3; 35:21), and the same letters read as "her tent", which no old copy has, are not noted. Not noted, as in Job 2 and Genesis 7 and 8: what the old Greek adds or puts differently (9:1 "and subdue it", from 1:28; 9:6 "I made" for "he made"; 9:10, where it does not have the last words, "to every living thing of the earth"; 9:16 "between me" for "between God"). At 9:6 the old Greek has "in exchange for his blood" where the Hebrew has ba'adam, which is the sense of the alt there. Also not noted: the change of 9:26 to "Blessed by Yahweh is Shem", which no old copy has.
- Two places where the Hebrew can be read in two ways have alts, each with an entry in the decision log: 9:6 ba'adam "by humankind" [alt: in exchange for that human being] (call: ask); 9:27 yaft "enlarge" [alt: persuade] (call: clear). Weighed and not bracketed: 9:2 the two phrases with be- taken with the first half of the verse, as more of those the fear is on (all four kinds are given into the hand either way); 9:4 basar benafsho damo as flesh cut from a living animal (the English is as open as the Hebrew); 9:5 lenafshotekhem as "for the sake of your lives", and ish achiv with the brother as the one who was killed ("from each one for his brother"), since either way a human life is demanded from the human being who took it; 9:10 mikkol ... lekhol as "from ... to"; 9:14-15 with the main clause beginning at "the bow will be seen"; 9:20 ish ha'adamah as what Noah began to be ("Noah began to be a man of the ground"); 9:22 "saw the nakedness of his father" as more than seeing (the words of Lev 18:7-8 and 20:17 are kept, so that the reader can hear them, and 9:23 says "did not see"); 9:24 haqqatan as "the younger"; 9:25-26 arur and barukh as wishes ("Cursed be", "Blessed be"), a second English wording; 9:26-27 lamo as "to them"; 9:27 the "he" of "may he live", which the English leaves as open as the Hebrew (Japheth, the one last named, or God, the subject of the verb before).
- 9:1-7 takes up 1:26-30, and the same Hebrew is the same English: peru urevu umil'u et-ha'arets "Be fruitful and multiply, and fill the earth" (1:28); kol-chayyat ha'arets "every wild animal of the earth" and kol-of hashamayim "every bird of the sky" (1:30); dege hayyam "the fish of the sea" (1:26, 28 have degat hayyam); lakhem yihyeh le'okhlah "will be yours for food" (1:29); yereq esev "green plants" (1:30 "every green plant"); natatti lakhem "I give you" (1:29); betselem elohim "in the image of God" (1:27, which has bara "create" where 9:6 has asah "make"). 9:7 sharats ba'arets "swarm on the earth", as at 8:17. we'attem set first (9:7) "And as for you" and wa'ani hineni with a participle (9:9) "And as for me, look, I am about to", as at 6:17, 21: 9:9 is built like 6:17, and 9:11 wahaqimoti et-beriti ittekhem "And I will establish my covenant with you" is the wording of 6:18; 9:17 haqimoti "I have established".
- mora "fear", the noun of yare (mora'akhem "the fear of you"); chat (9:2; Job 41:33) "terror": the noun of chittat "frighten" (Job 7:14). It shares its English with emah (Job 9:34), and "dread" is left for pachad, which stands beside mora at Deut 11:25. beyedkhem nittanu "into your hand they are given": yad singular as in the Hebrew, and the perfect of a thing done in the saying of it is an English present, like natatti (1:29; 9:3).
- ramas and remes in 9:2-3, as the Genesis 7 and 8 lines say: beside wild animals, birds and fish (9:2) "creep"; as the sum of them all (9:3 kol-remes asher hu-chay) "every moving thing that is alive". asher tirmos ha'adamah (9:2; Lev 20:25), where the ground is the subject of the verb, "that creeps on the ground", the English of 7:8: English does not say that the ground creeps with something (1:21 "with which the waters swarmed" has the same construction with sharats). keyereq esev (9:3) "like the green plants", with no verb added.
- natan "give" with le- or beyad (9:2, 3); with be- of a place (9:13 natatti be'anan) and with ben (9:12 noten beni uvenekhem) "set", as at 1:17 ("God set them in the vault of the sky"), which 9:13 calls to mind. It shares "set" with shit (4:25) and sim (4:15). In 9:12 "that I am setting" can go with the sign or with the covenant, in English as in Hebrew (17:2 has natan with berit). natatti (9:13) "I have set": the English perfect leaves open, as the Hebrew does, whether the bow is set there now or was there before and is now made the sign.
- akh (9:4) "Only", as at 7:23; we'akh (9:5) "And surely" (Job 16:7): "And only" would be heard as "nothing but". basar benafsho damo "flesh with its life, its blood": nefesh "life", as at Job 2:4, and damo set beside it with nothing added. lo tokhelu "you must not eat" (2:17; 3:1).
- darash with blood or a life as its object and miyyad (9:5; 42:22; Ezek 33:6) "demand ... from the hand of": "seek your blood", the listed rendering, would be heard as wanting to shed it. The verb stands three times in 9:5 and is "demand" each time. miyyad "from the hand of" all three times, also of an animal (4:11 "from your hand"). dimkhem lenafshotekhem "the blood of your lives": le- after a noun that has its own suffix is "of". kol-chayyah set against ha'adam (9:5) "every living thing", as chayyah is at 1:28 and 8:17. ish achiv "each one's brother".
- ha'adam five times in 9:5-6 "humankind", as in 6:1-7 and 8:21, so that the saying of 9:6 keeps its one word and "in the image of God he made humankind" is heard with 1:27. shafakh dam "shed blood" (shafakh is "pour out" at Job 12:21); the niphal "be shed". One bracketed note at 9:6 says that "blood" sounds like the word for "humankind". The halves of 9:6 keep the Hebrew order (shed, blood, humankind; humankind, blood, shed). asah with no subject named (9:6) "he made", though God is speaking, as the Hebrew has it.
- wayyomer ... lemor (9:8) "said ..., saying,", both verbs kept (8:15 has dibber "spoke"). zera of people (9:9) "offspring", as listed. 9:9-10 is one sentence. 9:10: be- before the members of the list is shown by dashes, as planned (7:21; 8:17), with behemah beside chayyat ha'arets "the livestock" and "every wild animal of the earth"; mikkol yots'e hattevah "of all that went out of the ark", min as at 6:19 and 7:22; lekhol chayyat ha'arets at the end, where it sums up, "to every living thing of the earth" (the same words are "wild animal of the earth" where they are one member of a list, as with 8:19 and 1:30).
- nikhrat of living things (9:11) "be cut off" (of a tree "be cut down", Job 14:7); it shares its English with bitstsa (Job 6:9). kol-basar stays "all flesh" under a negative (9:11 "never again will all flesh be cut off"), not "no flesh": it is the phrase of the whole story (6:12-13, 17; 7:21). lo ... od (9:11, twice; 9:15) "never again", as planned. mimme hammabbul "by the waters of the flood"; mabbul with no article (9:11, 15) "a flood". hayah le- (9:15) "become", as at 2:7; (9:13 wehayetah le'ot) "serve as", as at 1:14-15.
- ot habberit "the sign of the covenant" (9:12, 17); ot berit with no article (9:13; 17:11) "a sign of a covenant". olam after another noun "everlasting": ledorot olam (9:12) "for everlasting generations" and berit olam (9:16; 17:7, 13, 19) "an everlasting covenant", with no article, as in the Hebrew. It stands beside le'olam "forever" (3:22; 6:3) and shares its English with aidios (Jude 6).
- qeshet "bow", as at Job 20:24: the word for the weapon, and "rainbow" is not written (Ezek 1:28 "the bow that is in the cloud on a day of rain"). anan "cloud", as listed: be'anan "in the cloud", singular with the article all three times (9:13, 14, 16); innen anan (9:14) "bring cloud", and the cognate object is not shown (as at 8:21). nir'ah of the bow (9:14) "be seen", not "appear" (1:9; 8:5), so that it is heard with ur'itiha "and I will see it" (9:16). wehayah before a time clause (9:14) "And when", and the main clause is taken to begin at 9:15, "I will remember". zakar "remember", as planned (8:1). bekhol-basar (9:15, 16) "among all flesh", kept distinct from mikkol-basar "of all flesh" (6:19; 8:17). ben elohim (9:16) "between God", though God is speaking, as the Hebrew has it.
- Speeches: God speaks three times, each with its own "And God said" (9:1-7; 9:8-11; 9:12-16), and once more in 9:17; Noah's words are 9:25 and 9:26-27. Noah's sayings are verse, and each verse stays on one line (the Job 3 line).
- 9:18 weCham hu avi Khena'an "Now Ham was the father of Canaan": a sentence that breaks the chain (the Genesis 1-2 line). Shem weCham wayefet (9:18; 7:13) "Shem and Ham and Japheth", every "and" kept (5:32 and 6:10 have "and" once). Kena'an "Canaan".
- nafats (9:19) "scatter": another form of puts (10:18; 11:4, 8, 9), with the same English. kol-ha'arets of those who live on it (9:19) "the people of the whole earth", with "the people of" supplied, since English does not say that the earth scatters.
- wayyachel ... wayyitta (9:20) "made a beginning and planted": hechel "begin" (6:1), with the two verbs kept side by side, so that the English is as open as the Hebrew (he was the first to plant one, as hechel marks a first at 4:26 and 10:8, or it was the first thing he did). ish ha'adamah "the man of the ground", set beside the name: adamah stays "ground", so that it is heard with 5:29 and 8:21. nata "plant", as at 2:8; kerem "vineyard".
- yayin "wine", as listed; shatah min "drink some of" (min with food, 3:6); shakhar "become drunk", not softened (the same English as methysko). hitgallah (9:21; Prov 18:2) "uncover oneself", beside gillah "uncover" (Job 12:22), so that it can be heard with "uncover the nakedness of" (Lev 18:6-19). betokh (9:21) "inside".
- ervah "nakedness", beside arom and erom "naked" (2:25; 3:7): ervat aviv "the nakedness of his father", the same wording all three times (9:22, 23). higgid "tell", as listed, with no object, as in the Hebrew. bachuts "outside" (6:14 michuts).
- simlah (9:23) "cloak": the outer garment that is a covering by night (Exod 22:26-27; Deut 22:17); it is another form of salmah "clothes" (Job 9:31). A singular verb with two subjects that stand side by side (9:23) is put in ordinary English ("Shem and Japheth took"), as at John 2:2. shekhem "shoulder": al-shekhem shenehem "on the shoulders of the two of them" (shenehem as at 2:25; 3:7), the plural as English says it. achorannit "backward", both times; ufnehem achorannit "and their faces were turned backward", with "turned" supplied. kissah "cover", as listed. An object set first is kept first ("the nakedness of their father they did not see").
- yaqats (9:24) "awake", the same English as heqits (Job 14:12); miyyeno "from his wine", the picture kept. yada (9:24) "know", as at 8:11. beno haqqatan "his youngest son": haqqatan of one among more than two is "the youngest" (42:13; 1 Sam 16:11), and of one of two "the younger" (27:15). It agrees with the rest of Scripture: Shem was a hundred years old two years after the flood (11:10), so he was not the son born when Noah was five hundred (5:32; 7:6), and the order "Shem, Ham, Japheth" is not the order of birth. asah le- "do to".
- arur "Cursed is", the word of 3:14, 17 and 4:11, as planned; barukh (9:26) "Blessed is", the same construction. eved in 9:25-27 "slave": one who is made over to others (the Job 3 line; Josh 9:23 has arur and eved together of a people of Canaan); eved avadim "a slave of slaves", the form kept, like "song of songs". lamo (9:26, 27) "to him", as alemo is "on him" (Job 20:23). The jussives of 9:26-27 are "may".
- yaft (9:27; the hiphil of patah stands only here) "enlarge" [alt: persuade] (decision log, call: clear), with one bracketed note that it sounds like the name "Japheth". It will share "enlarge" with hirchiv where that verb has an object (Deut 12:20; 33:20); hirchiv le- (26:22) is to be "make room for". shakan (9:27) "live", as listed (Job 4:19), the same English as skenoo (John 1:14): it can be said of Japheth and of God. ohel "tent", as listed.
- 9:28-29 follows the wording of chapter 5: "And Noah lived three hundred and fifty years after the flood. And all the days of Noah were nine hundred and fifty years, and he died." achar hammabbul "after the flood".
- For Genesis 10: 10:1 elleh toledot "These are the generations of", as at 6:9, and achar hammabbul "after the flood" (9:28; 10:1, 32; 11:10); 10:8 hechel lihyot gibbor "began to be a mighty man" (hechel as at 6:1 and 9:20, gibbor as at 6:4); 10:18 nafotsu "were scattered", as at 9:19, kept distinct from nifredu (10:5, 32); mishpachah "family" (8:19); 10:21 achi Yefet haggadol has to be weighed with 9:24 ("his youngest son") and 11:10.

## Settled in Genesis 10
- Hebrew text: the Masoretic Text; the chapter has no Qere. The main text keeps to the Masoretic letters, vowels and accents: 10:10 kalneh is the name "Calneh" (the same letters with other vowels, "all of them", which no old copy has, are not noted); 10:14 keeps the Hebrew order, in which "from whom the Philistines came out" stands after the Casluhim (Amos 9:7 and Jer 47:4 bring the Philistines from Caphtor, and Deut 2:23 the Caphtorim; no old copy has another order here, so nothing is moved or noted); 10:21 the accents set achi apart and join Yefet haggadol. Two places where the old copies differ have alts, each with an entry in the decision log: 10:4 Dodanim "the Dodanim" [alt: the Rodanim, as the Samaritan copy and the old Greek have it here and the Hebrew text at 1 Chr 1:7] (call: clear); 10:24 "fathered Shelah" [alt: fathered Kenan, and Kenan fathered Shelah, as the old Greek has it here and at 11:12-13, with Luke 3:36] (call: clear). Not noted: a name that another copy, or the list of 1 Chronicles 1, gives in another form while nothing else in the sentence changes (10:3 Riphath, "Diphath" at 1 Chr 1:6; 10:23 Mash, "Meshech" in the old Greek and at 1 Chr 1:17, the name of Japheth's son at 10:2; 10:28 Obal, "Ebal" at 1 Chr 1:22); what the old Greek adds (10:2 an Elishah among the sons of Japheth; 10:22 a Kenan among the sons of Shem), as in Job 2 and Genesis 7 to 9; and 10:5, the proposal that "These are the sons of Japheth" has dropped out before "in their lands", which no old copy has.
- Two places where the Hebrew can be read in two ways have alts, each with an entry in the decision log: 10:11 yatsa Ashshur "he went out to Asshur" [alt: Asshur went out] (call: ask); 10:21 achi Yefet haggadol "the brother of Japheth the eldest" [alt: the older brother of Japheth] (call: ask). Weighed and not bracketed: 10:5 nifredu iyye haggoyim as "the coastlands of the nations were divided up" (10:32 has the same words with the nations themselves as the subject); 10:8 hechel lihyot as "was the first to be" (a second English wording); 10:9 lifne YHWH, where "before Yahweh" is as open as the Hebrew (in his sight, or to his face); 10:10 reshit as "the chief part" of his kingdom (the next verse goes on from it, and reshit mamlekhut is the start of a reign at Jer 26:1); 10:11 rechovot ir as "the open squares of the city" (it stands in a row of four cities, each with et, and ir has no article: a name, "Rehoboth-Ir"); 10:12 hi ha'ir haggedolah, where "that is the great city" leaves open, as the Hebrew does, whether it is Resen, Nineveh (Jonah 1:2) or the four together; 10:14 misham as the place the Philistines left or the people they come from ("from whom ... came out" serves both); 10:19 gevul as "territory"; 10:25 ha'arets as "the land", and what the dividing was (the English is as open as the Hebrew); 10:30 har haqqedem as a second limit or as another name for Sephar (a comma, as open as the Hebrew).
- Names take the form English has for them, and a name already published keeps its spelling: Shem, Ham and Japheth (5:32), Canaan (9:18), Havilah, Cush and Asshur (2:11-14), Uz and Sheba (Job 1:1, 15), Sodom and Gomorrah (Jude 7), and Mitsrayim "Egypt" (Deut 5:6), also where it is the name of Ham's son (10:6, 13): the Hebrew has one word for the man, the land and the people, and so does the English. Bavel (10:10; 11:9) "Babylon": the city's ordinary English name, so that it is the same word here as it will be in Kings and the Prophets; "Babel" is the same name in another spelling, and Obay can switch it. Ashshur is "Asshur" in 10:11 and in 10:22, one word as in the Hebrew (the land is "Asshur" at 2:14). Madai and Yawan (10:2, 4) "Madai" and "Javan", the names as English has them in this list; in later books the same two names stand for Media and Greece (2 Kgs 17:6; Dan 8:21), and whether they are then written as here or by the ordinary names of the lands is for those chapters, or for Obay. The same name stands twice, as in the Hebrew: Sheba (10:7, 28), Havilah (10:7, 29), Asshur (10:11, 22), and Lud (10:22) beside the Ludim (10:13).
- New names: Gomer, Magog, Madai, Javan, Tubal, Meshech, Tiras; Ashkenaz, Riphath, Togarmah; Elishah, Tarshish; Put; Seba, Sabtah, Raamah, Sabteca, Dedan; Nimrod; Erech, Accad, Calneh, Shinar; Nineveh, Rehoboth-Ir, Calah, Resen; Sidon, Heth; Gerar, Gaza, Admah, Zeboiim, Lasha; Eber; Elam, Arpachshad, Lud, Aram; Hul, Gether, Mash; Shelah, Peleg, Joktan; Almodad, Sheleph, Hazarmaveth, Jerah, Hadoram, Uzal, Diklah, Obal, Abimael, Ophir, Jobab; Mesha, Sephar.
- Peoples named in the plural with no article keep the Hebrew plural, as the Nephilim and the peoples of Deut 2:10-11 do, and take "the": the Kittim, the Dodanim, the Ludim, the Anamim, the Lehabim, the Naphtuhim, the Pathrusim, the Casluhim, the Caphtorim (Deut 2:23). Pelishtim "the Philistines", the name as English has it, like "Chaldeans" (Job 1:17).
